*Article: `3B1B — Tenet - Formal Execution.md` (Temporal Decision Architecture). Invert from a chosen future to the next optimal action. (Value function written **J**; **V** reserved for viability.)*

**Status: M-TEN's MATHEMATICS IS COMPLETE (2026-08-16, three slices). Δ(S), the inversion point, backward induction and the Controller join; then scenario ensembles, invariant actions, decision nodes and dead drops; then signal functions and the temporal pincer. The ONE open row is TEN-11 — the pawa-efficiency term — and it is parked on the economy, not on Tenet.**

| # | Feature | § | Rust status |
|---|---|---|---|
| TEN-1 | **Transition model `T: S×A→Δ(S)`** — a **probability distribution** over next states (MDP), not a single state | I·II | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`tenet.rs` · `Distribution`, `TransitionModel`) — a distribution that does not sum to 1 is **refused, not normalised** (normalising invents probabilities nobody declared, in the one input the optimisation reads as evidence). The R1 deterministic simulator is `Distribution::certain`, a **point mass** — a special case of this model rather than a competitor |
| TEN-2 | **Inversion point** `IP: S→{0,1}` — a precise target-state predicate that makes the inversion computable | III | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`InversionPoint`) — a declared predicate, **the viability kernel**, or both. The kernel reading is the one that matters: *"worth reaching"* becomes *"from here you can keep going"*, so the optimiser aims at survivability rather than at a number somebody picked. This is why SUS-10 had to land first |
| TEN-3 | **Scenario ensemble** `Ω={best, base, worst}` — each a `TransitionModel(p_…)`; `SimulateForward(s_0, T(ω), steps)` | IV | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`ensemble.rs` · `ModelTemplate`, `Scenario`, `Ensemble`) — a scenario is a **declared assignment of named probabilities** (`p_funding=0.85`), exactly as §IV writes it, pushed through one shared `ModelTemplate`. The three futures therefore differ **only** in their numbers over identical structure, which is the premise the whole comparison rests on. An unbound parameter is **named, never defaulted**; an incoherent one is refused by `Distribution::new`'s own rule. `Ensemble::new` **refuses fewer than two scenarios** — with one future every action is trivially invariant and nothing is ever a decision node |
| TEN-4 | **Backward induction (Bellman)** `J_t(s)=max_a[R+γΣP(s'|s,a)J_{t+1}(s')]` → value `J` + complete policy `π` (optimal action ∀ state×time) | V | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`backward_induct` → `Plan`) — sweeps from the terminal condition back, returning `J` **and a complete policy** (every state, every `t` — a policy answers off-path where a path cannot). `γ`/`R`/`H` are **declared spec fields**, and `Plan::horizon()` carries the window rather than implying forever. **The article's `V` is written `J`** per the Additions — `V` is the viable region everywhere else in the series. A dangling outcome is **refused**, not scored 0 |
| TEN-5 | **Invariant actions** `I(s_0)={a: π_A(s_0)=π_B(s_0)=π_C(s_0)}` — optimal across all scenarios = unconditional priorities | VI | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`EnsembleAnalysis::invariant_actions` → `InvariantSet`) — the intersection of the per-scenario optimal actions, run off TEN-4 unchanged, once per scenario. **The honest limit is structural, not a footnote:** `InvariantSet` carries the scenario names it was computed over and there is **no accessor returning the actions without them**, so *"this action is invariant"* full stop is unspellable. `the_one()` states §VI's at-most-one property (the article's own pseudocode intersects singletons) so nobody builds a picker over it |
| TEN-6 | **Decision nodes** `D={s: ∃ω_i,ω_j π_i(s)≠π_j(s)}` — where the optimal action diverges across scenarios | VII | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`decision_nodes`, `is_decision_node` → `DecisionNode`) — a node carries **what each scenario wants**, not merely that they disagree: the disagreement is the actionable part and is what a dead drop is written against. **A finding the fixture produced rather than confirmed:** divergence is **horizon-dependent** — a state splits the futures at `t=0,1` and not at `t=2,3`, because too near the end of the window no future can repay the costly move, so *"is this a decision node?"* is a question about a state **and a time** |
| TEN-7 | **Dead drops** — pre-committed `δ(D_i)=a*` chosen now under clarity; `ExecuteAtNode` falls back to `π(state)` off-node | VIII | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`DeadDropBook::pre_commit` / `execute_at` → `Resolution`) — Schelling's argument applied **to the pre-commitment itself**: a drop is refused now if it does not name a real decision node, names an action the model does not admit there, or carries a guard that does not parse — all obvious now, all expensive at the node. Guards reuse the existing predicate language (no new expression syntax). `Resolution` **never blends** a pre-commitment with a fallback: *"you decided this in advance"* and *"this is what the base scenario suggests"* carry different weight, the fallback is the **declared** base rather than a guess, and a drop that declined is **named**. A drop whose action no future proposed is **allowed and flagged** (`is_hedge`) — human judgment is permitted, invisibility is not |
| TEN-8 | **Signal functions** `σ_k: S^t→{0,1}` over the trajectory; `Monitor(history,signals)` fires → `TRIGGER Inversion` (HMM-style latent-scenario inference) | IX | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`pincer.rs` · `Trajectory`, `Signal`, `SignalMonitor`) — a predicate over the **trajectory**, as a small temporal layer (`Ever`/`Always`/`Never`/`At`/`Count` + connectives) over the single-state predicates already in the crate; no new expression language, no `eval`. **★ A correction to the article:** §IX's `IF σ(history) == 1` is two-valued, and read literally its own example *"no customers by week 6"* is **TRUE at week 0** — every warning fires on an empty history. `SignalVerdict` is three-valued (`Holds`/`Fails`/**`Undetermined`** with the step that settles it), early-determining in both directions; an **unbounded** `Never` can only ever be falsified, never confirmed. **Consumes `signal.rs` (MUL-15) as the firing mechanism**, and load-bearingly: a warning is persistent, so the refractory window is the **re-plan debounce** — a suppressed trigger is reported, not dropped. `MonitorReport::watched` carries the declared list, so *"nothing fired"* can never be quoted as *"nothing is wrong"* |
| TEN-9 | **Temporal pincer** — forward execution ∥ periodic backward recompute (reassess, update scenario probs, rerun BackwardInduct, refresh dead-drops, recalibrate signals) | X | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`run_pincer`) — all five backward steps real: a fired signal **reclassifies** the ensemble's base (§IX's HMM reading — an observable emission infers the latent scenario), `backward_induct` re-runs, and a dead drop whose node is no longer a decision node is **retired and named** rather than left aiming at a state every future now agrees about. `Recompute::policy_changed` is the observable proof a trigger did something. Runs on a **declared cadence** or on a signal. **Two disclosures:** "parallel" is **interleaved deterministically** — core has no threads, and that is §X's semantics rather than a simplification of it; and `T(s,a).sample()` takes its draw from an injected `Draws` (no RNG in core, same reason `approval.rs` takes `now`), which is also what makes a stochastic run reproducible enough to pin in a vector |
| TEN-10 | **Fork runs under the gate** — every simulated step passes the same `admit` gate; the sandbox is bounded by the household's own law | Add. | ✅ (R1 `simulate()` enforces the gate) |
| TEN-11 | **Pawa is the efficiency axis** — `Pawa(π,s)=Σ pawa(o,θ,s̃)`; `score(π)=Σ wᵢuᵢ(sim(π)) − λ·Pawa(π,s)` (optimal includes efficient) | Add. | 🔨 partial (per-branch pawa exists; ⏸️ full score ties to the economy) — **as of 2026-08-16 this is the ONLY open row in M-TEN**, and it is parked on the economy rather than on Tenet: every other row is built |
| TEN-12 | **Bellman = optimal control** — discrete case of min-accumulated-cost / stationary-action, **shared with the Controller (§3)** (Lyapunov/Pontryagin) | Add. | ✅ **joined 2026-08-16** (`rewards_from_region`) — with `R(s,a)=W(s)−E[W(s')]` the `H=1` sweep's `argmax J` **is** CTL-2's greedy `argmin W`. **One mechanism, not two that agree** — and a conformance vector *checks* it (computing the greedy choice independently) rather than asserting it. What the horizon buys is the ability to take a **worse first step for a better end**, which a one-step rule structurally cannot see |

**Summary:** **M-TEN's mathematics is complete as of 2026-08-16 — three slices took it from the single largest not-started module to one open row.**

*Slice 1, the optimiser core (TEN-1/2/4/12).* `Distribution`/`TransitionModel` made `T` a genuine **Δ(S)**, with the R1 forward simulator re-expressed as a point mass *inside* the model rather than beside it; `InversionPoint` made the terminal condition computable and it can be **the viability kernel** — *"worth reaching"* read as *"from here you can keep going"*, which is why SUS-10 had to land first; `backward_induct` returns `J` and a **complete policy**, with `γ`/`R`/`H` declared and the horizon carried; and `rewards_from_region` closes **TEN-12/CTL-12** — the `H=1` sweep **is** the Controller's greedy Lyapunov step, checked by a vector rather than asserted.

*Slice 2, the ensemble layer (TEN-3/5/6/7).* Runs that sweep **unmodified, once per declared scenario** and reads the disagreement. A scenario is a declared assignment of named probabilities pushed through one shared template, so the futures differ only in their numbers over identical structure. `I(s₀)` is the intersection, `D` the states where they split, and a dead drop is a decision taken **now, under clarity**, for a node not yet reached.

*Slice 3, the when-to-re-plan layer (TEN-8/9).* Signals are predicates over the **trajectory**, not the state — a small temporal layer over the crate's existing single-state predicates. The pincer runs forward on a sampled `T` and backward on a declared cadence or a fired signal: **reclassify → re-invert → refresh the dead drops**, with `policy_changed` as the observable proof a trigger did something. It **consumes the Signal primitive** (MUL-15) rather than defining a pulse, and load-bearingly: a warning condition is persistent, so §III's refractory window becomes the **re-plan debounce** — the reason MUL-15 was worth building first.

**Honest limits are carried in the types rather than the prose.** Invariance holds only over the **declared** scenarios (`InvariantSet` has no accessor returning the actions without the names); a single-scenario ensemble is **refused**, because one future makes every action trivially invariant; `Resolution` never blends a pre-commitment with a policy fallback; `MonitorReport::watched` means *"nothing fired"* can never be read as *"nothing is wrong"*; and every `Recompute` states its horizon, because re-running the sweep more often does not make it see further.

**Three article defects were levelled up rather than copied**, each recorded in the module docs and the vectors: §V's value function is written **`J`, not `V`** (the Additions' rename — `V` is the viable region everywhere else, including in the Lyapunov term this joins to); a distribution that does not sum to 1 is **refused rather than normalised**; and §IX's two-valued `σ(history)==1` is **three-valued**, because read literally its own example *"no customers by week 6"* is true at week 0 and every warning fires before the run begins. **Two findings came from running the fixtures rather than predicting them:** decision nodes are **horizon-dependent**, and a costly first step can be right at `H=3` and wrong at `H=1`.

**Still ⬜:** only **TEN-11**, the pawa-efficiency term — per-branch pawa exists and the full `score(π)` ties to the economy, so it is parked *there* rather than here. **The spine reads: Monitor observes ✅, Tenet models ✅, Controller decides ✅.** Its 4th sub-module (S/T "fundamental forces") = M-SUS (S) + M-OP (T), already built.

#### M-OPV · Operative (Symbiont)  ✅ read
*Article: `3B1B — Operative - Formal Execution.md` (SCOUT). Agents over the **same** ⟨S,T⟩, differing only in `u_i`. "A shared world, N preferences, a search, a sandbox, an aggregation rule, and a lock the agents do not hold."*

**Status: the most-built module after Sustain-design — in PYTHON. In Rust: the gate half exists (sandbox-under-same-gate, privilege), and as of 2026-08-16 the AGENT LAYER IS OPEN and its BOUNDARY IS BUILT — `ω` + the utility vector (OPV-1, OPV-3), the Goodhart guard (OPV-27), the presentation duty (OPV-29) and the sparse MoE router (OPV-28) — so §XV's three duties are all at least partial. ★★★ **As of 2026-08-17 THE DEEP BUNDLE IS CLOSED and M-OPV has no unstarted rows**: `Π` (OPV-4), the two attentions (OPV-7), `M_self`/`M_world` (OPV-11) and learning (OPV-6) are all built. Three of the four were reference-less, and each leaned on an internal constraint instead of a parity grep — `Π`'s minting, the salience family's four closed surfaces, `ensemble`'s declared-structure split, and OPV-3's refusal to scalarise. ★★★ **And as of 2026-08-18 that residual is DISCHARGED — `ω` is assembled** (`agent.rs`). The four faculties are on a reasoning agent and, the whole test of the slice, **every one is read on a real path**: `Agent::act` scans with **attention**, lets a fired `Reframing` **end the turn before `Π` runs**, makes the top-scoring focus `Π`'s own `trigger_event`, and runs a meme from the agent's **library** through the real gate; `Agent::learn_from` runs a full **vary/select/retain** round over that same library; `Agent::self_model` **derives** `M_self`. ★★★ They are **required, not `Option`al** — an agent without attention would be OPV-7's half-mind and is unrepresentable — and `Agent` holds **no `Shared`**, so OPV-1's Props are untouched (proven behaviourally: the same agent handed a narrower world refuses a variation naming a move that world lacks). ★★★ `M_world` went to **`Omega`, not the agent** — §VII's *one per household* — and is read by `Omega::agreement_over`, so OPV-11's keystone now fires off real population state.

**★★★ Update 2026-08-18 — follow-on wiring 2: `ω` now reasons UNDER A WARRANT, and OPV-4's `Unchecked` residual is discharged** (`agent.rs`, `strategy::run_under`). ★★★ **The gap, confirmed in code first**: `strategy::run_with_effect` passed `&Authorization::Unchecked` to `execute_admitted` on **every** node, and wiring 1's `Agent::act` called it — so an agent reasoned **outside the very authorization conjunct IMM-7 was built for**. OPV-4 had named this exact residual when it shipped. ★★★ **This is the confused deputy in its designed place.** A strategy acts on a household's authority over inputs — a trigger, a meme, a correction — it did not choose: Hardy's case exactly. `Authorization::Capability` carries **no `&Memberships`**, so a strategy under one cannot fall back on the issuer's ambient authority even by mistake (Miller–Yee–Shapiro's *no ambient authority*, as a property of the type), and `Agent::act` takes the `Warrant` as a **required parameter** — there is no route to `Unchecked` through an `Agent`. ★★★ **Least privilege by provenance, where OPV-6 meets IMM-7**: `authority_for` attenuates the household's capability to `Rights::Only(that meme's own moves)` plus a tier ceiling from `MemeTrustPolicy`, keyed on `MemeProvenance` — so an **imported** meme (NFL: worth another household's non-uniformity, not yours) runs weaker than an authored one and is refused **on tier alone**, the same discipline IMM-10 gave a learned parse rule. **Nothing was rebuilt** — `Capability`/`Attenuation`/`Rights` are IMM-7's, and a strategy is simply a new holder. ★★ **Two real refusals**: at **attenuation**, a strategy naming a move the household lacks yields `Amplification::Rights` and **no warrant at all** (stronger than refusing node by node); at the **gate**, a warrant cut for one meme running another, or aimed at another target, refuses with state **byte-untouched**. ★★ **The `EffectClass` reconcile**: `select` already ran `Sandbox` and still does; `act` runs `Sandbox` too, and that is **structural** — a live effect needs an `ApprovalToken` bound to **one** `(operator, params)` pair and single-use, while a strategy is many nodes, so one token cannot admit a walk, `act` takes none, and **an agent cannot fire a live effect**. Making an outcome real stays the human's approve-and-execute path (OPV-30/31), one act at a time. ★ Prop 3 holds and now nests: `T` at build, the warrant at the gate. ★ Naming: `WarrantError`, not `AuthorityError` — `editing::AuthorityError` answers whether a *definition edit* was authorised. **Thirty-third collision.**

Das & Dennis (1997) exactly: *collapsing to a scalar does not hide a trade-off, it deletes options.* A **convex companion case** is swept too, so the finding reads as *scalarisation misses exactly the non-convex ones* rather than *scalarisation never works*. `Δu_i(o,s) = u_i(o(s)) − u_i(s)` is **transition-shaped** as §4E's `D(s,s')` is, and still a vector. A dimension a point does not carry is **refused, not read as zero** — zero is a value. ★ **Nash recorded precisely, in the reference's favour and against it:** `nash_utility_score` is genuinely correct for the degenerate 1-D case and genuinely tested (4 tests) — **AT PARITY**, as §II says. Two nuances recorded rather than glossed: (1) the disagreement point is **named in three docstrings** (council.py, curator.py, protege.py) and **subtracted in none** — the arithmetic is `exp(mean(ln u))` over raw utilities, so a party gaining *exactly nothing* zeroes Nash's product (`NoBargain`) and does not dent a geometric mean, which is the single property the product is most known for; (2) it is **not on a production decision path** — the only non-test references are its own definition and one docstring, while `aggregate_delegated_votes` (confidence-weighted) is what actually runs, so *at parity* must not be read as *in the loop*. ★ **A tension found while grounding, stated not resolved by fiat:** Nash takes **one scalar per party**, so applying it to vector utilities requires the very collapse §II warns against — hence frontier **first**, losslessly, and bargain among frontier points only if a single pick is genuinely required, which is why *present the frontier, not the winner* is its own duty (OPV-29) |

A **shape** worth naming, not a live hole. ★ **The cycle guard reports rather than truncates:** both sides bound the walk identically, but the reference then falls out of the loop and builds a proposal from the last result, so **a cyclic graph and a finished one look alike to the caller**. `WalkOutcome::StepsExhausted` makes them different. ★ One small real difference recorded rather than rounded away: the reference's dynamic resolver recurses into nested **dicts but not lists**; this resolves both. **Residuals:** the walk **does not build a proposal** (that is presentation, and OPV-1's `Proposal` already exists); ★★ ★★★ **RESIDUAL DISCHARGED 2026-08-18 (follow-on wiring 2, `agent.rs` + `strategy::run_under`)** — the residual read *authorization and effects are passed `Unchecked` ... the honest residual to name, since a strategy is exactly the deputy IMM-7's capability was built for*, **and that is exactly what was built**. `run_under` takes an `&Authorization`; `run`/`run_with_effect` delegate to it with `Unchecked`, so every prior caller and every R1 parity vector is byte-for-byte unchanged — but `Agent::act` takes a **`Warrant` as a required parameter**, so *there is no route to `Unchecked` through an `Agent` at all*. ★★★ `Agent::authority_for` cuts the warrant from the household's capability by **attenuation only**: `Rights::Only(that meme's own moves)` — least privilege even for an authored strategy — plus a **tier ceiling keyed on the meme's `MemeProvenance`** (`MemeTrustPolicy`, a deliberate mirror of `learned::TrustPolicy`: declare all three, no `Default`). So an **imported** meme runs weaker than an authored one and is refused on tier alone, which is where OPV-6's provenance and IMM-7's attenuation meet. ★★ **Two real refusals**: a strategy naming a move the household's own capability lacks is refused **at attenuation** (`Amplification::Rights` — *no warrant is issued at all*, stronger than refusing node by node), and a warrant cut for meme *A* running meme *B*, or aimed at another target, is refused **at the gate** (`capability_carries` / `NotDesignated`) with state **byte-untouched**. ★ Prop 3 is untouched and now nests: `T` at **build**, the warrant at the **gate**. *(Original text, kept: authorization and effects were passed `Unchecked`, since **a strategy is exactly the deputy IMM-7's capability was built for**.)*; `typecheck` validates shape, not acyclicity (the step limit is the substitute, and is named as one); first-matching-edge means **edge order carries meaning**; and state threads, so a mid-walk refusal leaves earlier commits in place. |

| OPV-6 | **Learning = search over meme space** `M(t+1)=retain(select_{u,Viab}(vary(M(t))))`; steps on **results, not a clock**; **No Free Lunch** ⇒ libraries are per-operative and per-Sustain, provenance is part of a meme's meaning | IV | ✅ **done 2026-08-17** (`learning.rs`) — **the LAST row of the deep bundle, and of the four-phase plan.** Reference-less for *strategy* learning, but see the counterweight: it is not a nil one. ★★★ **STEP-0's sharp question, and the answer scopes the row: the one place structure moves is the STRATEGY LIBRARY, not `M_world`.** OPV-11 shipped `structure_is_declared()` returning `true` for every value and named that as its honesty rather than a stub. That stays true, and the two are consistent because they are about **different objects**: a world model's causal structure is declared — only the residual, a `Scenario`'s probabilities, is bound from data, and rewriting `T̂` from observations would be a far larger claim than §VII makes — while the **memes**, each of which is a `Π` from OPV-4, are exactly what §IV's vary/select/retain moves. **Learning changes the operative, not the world it believes in.** ★★★ **Proposition 3 survives variation, by inheritance rather than a second check.** `vary` rebuilds a strategy through the **same public constructors**, so every node still holds a `LegalMove` minted from `Shared` — there is no path here that builds a node from a bare string. A `VaryOp::Extend` naming a move outside `T` comes back in `Varied::refused` as `MoveNotInT`, which is how the bound is **observable** rather than merely true; and the positive half asserts the variant's move set is a subset of `Shared::moves()` itself, not of a copy. ★★ **`select` returns a FRONTIER, never a winner.** `u_i` is a vector and OPV-3 gave `Utility` no scalar method on purpose. Selection scores each variant and returns `pareto_frontier` over the viable ones — OPV-29 calls presenting only the top-ranked option *a presentation bug with the consequences of an architecture bug*; here it would be an **architecture bug outright**, because the survivor is what the library **keeps**. ★★ **The venue is the real sandbox**: `run_with_effect(..., EffectClass::Sandbox)` — the full §4E gate, §X's fork-and-replay under the *same* rules — so a variant that breaches the household's own invariant comes back `Fitness::Refused` and never reaches the frontier. **A bad score, not an error.** ★ And a run that finishes but leaves a dimension `u_i` reads absent is `Fitness::Unscorable` — **no score, not zero**, matching `Utility::at`'s own refusal and the `Skew::Unknown` / `Soundness::Unavailable` / `EffectiveN::BoundedAbove` family. ★ Isolation is **by construction, not by mechanism** — the core is pure, the walk gets a clone, the result is read and dropped; nothing was protected from a write because there was no write to protect from, and that is said plainly rather than claimed as a sandbox feature. ★★ **Steps happen on RESULTS, not on a clock — structurally.** `Feedback` has exactly §IV's three variants (`Correction`, `PredictionDiverged`, `Objection`) and **no `Scheduled`**; `learn` takes one by value with no overload that omits it, so a host with a scheduler still has to name the result it is reacting to. ADR-0001 makes it doubly true: the core has no clock to trigger a round with. ★ And `Feedback::from_projection` returns **`None` when the projection and the observation agree** — *a learning step with no triggering result is a no-op*, said by the constructor rather than left to discipline; a divergence carries the **horizon**, because OPV-11's `Projection` cannot be quoted without one. ★★★ **"Operatives are attractors" is INSTRUMENTED, never asserted.** §4G.1 reads operatives as attractors of the iteration; there is no theorem here that it converges, that fixed points are unique, or that reaching one is good — **the evolutionary literature supplies the mechanism, Sustena supplies the scoring, and neither supplies a guarantee**. So `Stabilisation::proves_convergence()` is **`false` for every value**, present so the claim can be asserted *absent* rather than merely left unwritten — the same device as `structure_is_declared` always being `true`. What *is* built is the instrument: `LearningTrace` records the rounds, `stabilisation()` reports trailing rounds that changed nothing (with `Unobserved` as the third answer — *nothing has been observed* is not *nothing changed*), `retained_fitness()` answers whether the survivors actually scored better, and `triggers()` reads the results-not-a-clock rule back off the record. ★★★ **No Free Lunch, made structural.** Averaged over all objectives every search is identical (Wolpert–Macready), so a library is worth exactly **this** household's non-uniformity. `MemeLibrary` is scoped to one `(sustain, operative)` pair, `LibraryScope` has **no `Default`** (a scopeless library would be the global pool NFL says cannot exist), `Meme` has **no public constructor**, and `MemeProvenance` is **minted only by the library** — `import` is the only door for a foreign strategy and it **always** stamps `Imported { origin }`. **A stranger's meme cannot be written down as `Authored`**, the `Capability`/`Score`/`VisualSpec` mechanism applied to a claim about where a strategy came from. ★ Importing from your own scope is refused (`SameScope`: nothing crossed a boundary), and an imported meme **drops the donor's `Fitness`** — a score earned against another household's problems is not a score here, so a borrowed meme arrives unproven. ★★ **Counterweight, and it is real: the reference RUNS this loop one layer down.** `parse_rule_learn.py` + `parse_rule_proposer.py` + `add_parse_rule`/`modify_parse_rule`/`retire_parse_rule` are a genuine vary/verify/retain over **ParseRules** — `synthesize_rule_from_correction` is deterministic template synthesis (explicitly not an LLM, the same discipline this row adopted for its own reason), `verify_proposed_rule` is a real two-condition `V(r)` with **both** required, and `parse_rule_edits` (`active`/`superseded`/`retired`) is **append-only versioned**. ★★ And it is genuinely **feedback-triggered** — a human's confirmed capture correction, never a schedule. *The results-not-a-clock discipline is the reference's own practice; this row declared it rather than discovering it.* ★★★ **The finding is what it has no analogue of:** (1) selection there is a **corpus regression check, not a fitness** — nothing runs a candidate under the gate and asks what state it produced, so `select_{u,Viab}` has no counterpart; and (2) **there is no NFL scope** — `parse_rule_edits` is not per-Sustain, and `ParseRule.provenance` is a plain **string** a caller can set to anything, including whatever makes an import read as native. ★ The reference is also honest about its own gap: `propose_and_verify(generator=None)` ships with no generator wired. ★★ **Greps carried:** `vary`, `meme`, `evolutionary`, `holland`, `wolpert`, `attractor`, `fitness` and *no free lunch* are all **grep-0**; and the sharpest false positive in this row by count is **`variant` — 135 hits, every one the substring inside `invariant`**. `library` (32) is the Arena package-marketplace panel; `provenance` (45) is `ParseRule.provenance` and `/orchie/balance-provenance`; `select_` (7) is SQLAlchemy's `select_from`. ★ **A real defect caught by a test rather than by inspection:** a variant's id was the parent plus the op's *label*, so two `Optimise` ops on the same node and parameter minted the **same id** — two genuinely different variants silently conflated, one scored and the other reported *already in the library*. Fixed at the root: the id is `parent/index:label` (unique by construction) and the label now carries the **value**, because *retuned `amount`* and *retuned `amount` to 250* are different facts and only the second says what was learned. An identity and a description are different jobs. **Residuals:** ★ the three vary operators are the article's three and **no crossover** between two memes; ★ `retain` is **append-only** — nothing is ever removed, so **forgetting is a separate decision from learning** and this row does not make it; ★★ **extending at the exit moves the exit**, a chosen semantic rather than a derived one (the alternative left the new node unreachable, growing `moves()` without changing behaviour — the inert-addition failure this build refuses elsewhere); ★ selection scores the **end state only**, so two variants reaching the same state score identically; ★★ **nothing wires a library onto `ω` yet**, for the fourth time and the same reason — *a declared-but-inert field reads as built*; and ★ **no cost is charged for a round**, which OPV-7's `B_att` and the pawa meter would both have opinions about — parked with `κ` and the juul balance. |
| OPV-7 | **Two attentions, inside EVERY operative** `⟨narrow, broad⟩`, both non-empty; narrow returns a **score**, broad returns a **re-framing trigger**; a council of one-attention agents is a **council of half-minds** | V | ✅ **done 2026-08-17** (`attention.rs`) — **reference-less, so the counterweight is internal consistency rather than a parity grep.** ★★★ **STEP-0's constraint, and it is the one that mattered: narrow must NOT become a fifth notion of *important*.** The salience family closed rival-importance on four surfaces — brightness (MON-7), score (UI-1/UI-7), position (UI-13), exemption (CTL-8) — and a **scoring** attention is exactly where a fifth could open. So `Score` has **no public constructor and no public fields**; the only way to obtain one is `narrow_scan`, which computes it from `Region::distance` — the household's own `d(s,V)` — and nowhere else. An operative cannot mint a score, cannot raise its own, and has **no field anywhere** to declare a rival metric. ★ The absence is checked too: an unreadable state yields **no score**, not a fabricated zero. ★ Same discipline one size down: `Aperture::stance()` reads narrow-vs-broad **off ⟨b,d,ρ⟩**, so an operative cannot call a broad aperture narrow by saying so — which matters, because *both non-empty* is enforced by comparing stances. ★★★ **Both attentions, structurally:** `Attention` has **two required fields, no `Option`, no single-attention constructor** — the half-mind is unrepresentable. And a pair landing in the **same region** is refused (`NotTwoRegions`): declaring one aperture under two names is the narrow/broad partition arriving through the back door, and §V is explicit that both belong to **one** mind. ★★ **Two output types, not two scores:** narrow returns a `Score`, broad returns a `Reframing` — *the frame you were scoring in has changed*, with **no magnitude**, because a number there would invite a caller to weigh it against a narrow score. That collapse is precisely what makes an agent a maximiser inside a frame it cannot notice is wrong (Ashby; §XIV's Goodhart drift arriving through the attention layer). ★ And broad **ignores what the task was already looking at**, so it reports what narrow is *not* covering rather than firing on everything narrow saw. ★★★ **A path with fringes, and the subtree is unspellable:** each level samples up to `b` siblings as fringes and descends into exactly **one**, so the walk is linear in `d`. There is **no `walk_subtree`** — the geometric traversal is not merely discouraged, it is not expressible. `subtree_visits()` exists **only to measure the gap** and nothing consumes it: at `b=8, d=6` the subtree is **over thirty thousand** visits against a path of **48**. ★★ **The closed trade, as arithmetic:** `κ·b·d·ρ`, so `⟨1,4,3⟩` and `⟨12,1,1⟩` both cost 12 — deep-and-narrow and broad-and-shallow are the **same spend**, and doubling depth breaks a budget until resolution pays for it. **No free axis.** The bound applies to the **pair**, since an operative attends with both. ★★ **Depth is over the real ⊕ tree** — `MonitorEngine`'s parent relation, the same structure `holarchy::escalate` climbs; escalation goes up, attention goes down, and **no parallel tree was introduced** (children are derived from the existing parent chain). ★ **And the footprint reconciles with UI-1**: pawa bounds the **scan**, `K ≈ 4` bounds the **hold** — *anything can scan widely and hold four; nothing can hold widely.* ★ **Counterweight, and it is the reference's:** the cheap-scan/costly-look split is **its own working discipline**, documented in `operatives/base.py` (*'`should_evaluate()` is ALWAYS cheap: threshold check only, zero LLM calls'*) and honoured. This row makes the shape a type and the width a bounded quantity; **it did not discover the shape.** ★★ Greps worth carrying: `attention` returns **51 hits in the reference and none about this** — every one is *needs-attention*, the Monitor's queue, which is the **object** of attention rather than the faculty; and `narrow`/`broad` return 42 between them, all English adjectives. **Residuals:** `B_att` is **a number, not a balance** — the cost function and bound are real, `κ` is **declared and uncalibrated** (the `pawa_meter` position, in the same words), and wiring it to a juul balance is parked with the economy; the stance boundary `b > d·ρ` is a **declared heuristic**, named as one rather than presented as derived; the path descends into the **first** child (a most-urgent-child descent is a real follow-on); broad reports the **first** frame change rather than enumerating all of them; and ★★ **nothing calls attention from an operative yet** — `ω` still has no `attention` field, for the same reason it had no `Π` slot: *a declared-but-inert field reads as built*. ★★★ **ADDENDUM 2026-08-18 — `B_att` IS NOW A BALANCE, AND TWO OF THIS ROW'S RESIDUALS ARE CLOSED.** (1) *`B_att` is a number, not a balance* — **closed** by `AttentionBudget` (`attention.rs`): `spent` is the **fold** of an append-only spend list and `remaining` is `allocated − spent`, so **`spent + remaining == allocated` holds by the shape of the type**, asserted as an equation **at every step** of a real sequence rather than once at the end. An unaffordable attention is **refused** and **nothing is appended** (the budget is compared byte-for-byte, not just by its remaining figure). ★★★ **Refused, never clamped — and that is structural, not taste.** `stance()` classifies by `b > d·ρ`, so lowering `ρ` or `d` **can flip a Narrow aperture to Broad**, and a clamp could therefore silently produce the **one-region-twice** attention `Attention::declared` exists to forbid. Proven by a case that flips a real stance, not argued. An unaffordable turn returns `Reasoning::AttentionUnaffordable` — the **fifth honest outcome**, distinct from `NothingInView`, because an exhausted budget and an empty household are different facts. ★★ **`spend` takes an `Attention`, not an amount** — the mirror of `charge(&PawaReading)`/`issue(&PawaReading)` one layer up, and each record **names the attention it paid for**. ★★ **Charged once for the PAIR**: billing the two apertures separately would let a turn afford narrow, spend it, then fail on broad — the exact half-mind this row's two-required-fields rule exists to prevent. (2) *Nothing calls attention from an operative yet* — **closed earlier**, by the agent-wiring slice (`Agent` holds `⟨narrow, broad⟩`; `Agent::act` runs both scans). Corrected here because it was still recorded as open: **a stale limit is worse than a missing one**. `Agent::act_within` now charges `B_att` **before either scan runs**, with `act` delegating via `AttentionSpending::Unbudgeted` so every prior caller is byte-identical (the delegating-variant pattern's **fourth** use). ★★ **And `κ_att` is now GOVERNED** (PAWA-11): the module constant was **demoted** to `Parameters::GENESIS_ATTENTION_KAPPA` and **`Aperture::cost()` removed outright** — **no path prices an aperture from a constant**. Genesis is `1.0`, exactly the constant it replaced, so the whole pre-existing suite passes unchanged (that is the parity evidence). ★ Two consequent deletions, the same move one level down: `Scan.cost: usize` → `Scan.aperture` + `cost_under`; and `SelfModel`'s `attention_budget: usize` — which stored the **cost** and called it the **budget**, a naming defect only visible once a real budget existed — **removed rather than renamed**. ★ **What is deliberately NOT governed: the allocation.** How much attention *this* operative gets *this* period legitimately differs between operatives, so a single global scalar would be the wrong shape (the same judgement PAWA-8 made about its `Schedule` type); it is host-supplied per operative. **Residuals still standing:** ★★ `κ_att` is **still declared and uncalibrated**, and **`B_att` is still not denominated in juul** — bridging needs `κ_att` and `κ_c` calibrated *against each other*, which neither is, so an exchange rate today would be a made-up number between two declared ones; ★★ `ParameterSpec` expresses **bounded, not positive**, so a governed `κ_att` of **zero** makes every scan free and the budget vacuous (note the asymmetry with `issuance_rate`, where zero is a *safe* policy while here it *disables an enforcement mechanism*) — it would at least be a **visible** change rather than a source edit; ★ **no period** (the core has no clock, so `renew` is the host saying one turned); and ★ the scan's footprint **still does not feed** `compose_view`'s candidate pool. |
| OPV-8 | **Attention meter = breadth × depth × resolution** over the ⊕ tree; `pawa(a)=κ_a·b·d·ρ ≤ B_att`; **iso-cost** — deep-and-narrow and broad-and-shallow are the same spend; **the working set is attention's footprint** (scan priced here, hold is §4H's K≈4) | V | ⏸️ **κ_a is a declared coefficient with no measurement wired to it** — `pawa_meter` charges over mutation/event/constraint-eval counts and bytes, **nothing keyed to breadth, depth or sampling rate**. The Note's own framing: *the system has the **hold** (knapsack, `DEFAULT_BUDGET = 4`, built) without the priced **scan** that fills it* — the inverse of the usual gap, and a small seam to close from both sides |
| OPV-8b | **★ κ_t — the LLM token term — is the meter's missing half, and both halves already exist on separate paths.** `pawa_meter.py` ships `1.0·compute + 0.01·storage` with **no token term**; meanwhile `_call_claude` already writes `model`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `llm_tokens_used` per call to `operators_log`. *"Both halves of the measurement exist on two paths joined by nothing; adding κ_t and routing token counts into the meter is small, well-defined work"* — and it is **the whole metering half of §4G.7 item 6**. (The **charging** half does not exist: `PawaLedger.charge()` is scaffolded and unwired.) Related: **model selection is a Python default argument** (`model: str = HAIKU` in `operatives/base.py`, passed explicitly at every call site) — *exactly the shape item 6 says it should not be* | Note · XII | ⬜ **not started** — the single cheapest item in this module, and it needs no new machinery |

| OPV-10 | **Sub-operative = ⊕, not a new primitive** — `Σ_ω = (⊕_j Σ_ω'_j) ⊕ Σ_ω^own`, `V_ω'` within `V_ω`, admittance composes along the holon path; **Corollary 2 — depth adds conjuncts and cost, never power** | VI | 🔨 the ⊕/admit-along-path machinery ✅ in Rust; **the agent-layer instance ⬜** (Python's sub-operatives are graph *file paths*, so ⊕ here is named, not structural) |
| OPV-11 | **`M_self` is a Sustain; `M_world ≈ Σ̂`** — Conant–Ashby: a good regulator **is** a model; Sustena's M_world starts **declared** (only the residual is learned) — easier and more brittle, "wrong in ways it cannot notice"; **one M_world per household = the Tenet**, forked per councillor | VII | ✅ **done 2026-08-17** (`models.rs`) — **reference-less** (`M_self`, `M_world`, `conant`, `ashby` all grep-0 there), so the counterweight is internal consistency. ★★★ **STEP-0 changed what this row IS: `M_world`'s engine was already built here, in two pieces.** `tenet::TransitionModel` is `S × T → Δ(S)`, and — the one that matters — **`ensemble::ModelTemplate` + `Scenario` ARE already §VII's declared-structure / learned-residual split**: the template declares *which edges exist*, the scenario binds *the probabilities on them*, `instantiate` combines. So `WorldModel` is a **framing** that adds exactly two things — a **fidelity claim** and an **identity** — and the identity is the load-bearing one, because it is what makes the correlation below **detectable at all**. This row did not build a second engine and does not claim to. ★★★ **The sharp question, resolved structurally: `M_world` is `Σ̂`, an approximate COPY.** `Ŝ`/`V̂`/`T̂` are **authored** and merely *consistent with* `Shared` — never a handle on it — so an operative never holds the world it models. `agrees_with` is therefore a **report** (`imagined` — moves the model believes in that `T` does not have; `unmodelled` — moves `T` has that the model is blind to), **not a constructor guard**: a model is *allowed* to be wrong, and §VII's whole point is that it is wrong *in ways it cannot notice*, which a guard would hide by refusing to build it. `blind_to(schema)` names the dimensions outside `Ŝ` as **unregulated** — Ashby's remainder, stated. ★★ **`M_self` composes `ω`'s own parts rather than forking them** — `SelfModel::of(&Operative, &StrategyGraph, &Attention, boundary)` reads objectives off `u`, moves off `Π`, budget off `⟨narrow, broad⟩`. Nothing is copied that could drift. ★★★ And **CELL's recursion is proven, not asserted**: `moves_within(&Shared)` holds because `Π`'s nodes were minted from `Shared` in the first place — the inner Sustain is bounded by the outer world **by construction**. ★★★ **THE KEYSTONE — the shared-model correlation, made honest.** `EffectiveN::{Independent(n), BoundedAbove{headcount, shared_model}}`. `Agreement::independent(n)` **takes no model** and `Agreement::via_shared_model(n, &m)` **cannot yield `Independent`**, so *five councillors who forked one `M_world` agreeing* and *five independent observers agreeing* are **different values of different shapes** — the masquerade is unspellable rather than discouraged, and the same headcount reads differently **by provenance alone**. ★★★ **And no number is fabricated**: `BoundedAbove` carries the headcount and the model's id and **no estimate**, because the shortfall depends on how wrong the shared model is, which *nobody inside it can measure*. Forking gives independence of **sampling**, not of **assumptions**, and the correlation **does not dilute with headcount** (2, 20, 200 — still one model). ★ **Counterweight, and the finding is real:** `council.py` aggregates as **`weighted_yes = Σ confidence for YES votes`** — summing confidences across councillors is exactly the arithmetic §VII names. But the reference **forks per councillor first** (Slice 7.3, unique fork ids, none shared) — it has the *sampling* independence, and it did that before anyone framed the problem; and it **never claims** assumption independence anywhere, so the shortfall there is **unnamed rather than misstated**. ★★ Greps carried so nobody re-derives them: `fidelity` **6 hits, all unrelated English**; `effective` **10, all unrelated**; `model` very many real hits, **none about `M_self`/`M_world`** (they are ML/ORM/data-model uses). **Residuals, named:** ★★ `structure_is_declared()` **is always `true`** — that is the row's honesty, not a stub: structure is *never* revised by data here, and a method that could return `false` would imply a learning path that does not exist. ★★ **The fidelity claim is declared, never measured** — `Fidelity::claimed` demands a **non-empty basis** and a value in `[0,1]`, and the **gate never reads it** (`execute` takes no model, so a claim cannot reach an admission — structural, not promised). ★ `Projection` has **no value-only accessor**: `reported() -> (f64, usize)` hands back the horizon with the number, so a projection cannot be quoted without the horizon it is only valid over. ★ **Nothing wires either model onto `ω` yet** — the same restraint as `Π` and `attention`: *a declared-but-inert field reads as built*. ★ **`⊕̂` is not modelled** — a model of composition is a further row. |
| OPV-12 | **Forks buy independence of SAMPLING, not of ASSUMPTIONS** — every fork forks one shared M_world, so residual correlation presents as **unanimity**; effective N < headcount by an amount nobody inside can measure | VII·XIII | ✅ **DONE** — `core/independence.rs`, 11 tests (`WorldModel` is the `M_world` the row was waiting on). ★★★ **`effective_n` is documented and named as a CEILING, never a measurement** (`at_most()`, so a caller reading it aloud says the qualification) — distinct models may still share assumptions and nobody inside can say by how much, which is §XIII's own words; a number presented as a measurement here would be the failure it is reporting. ★★★ **A unanimous single-model council reads as `TheModelTalking`, not as evidence** — the dangerous case is the one that looks best. ★★ **Separate forks are proven not to fix it**: a test gives three councillors three genuinely distinct sandboxes and the effective N is still 1. ★★ A reused fork is reported as a **separate, cruder** failure — sharing a model is subtle, sharing a sandbox means they did not even reason separately. |
| OPV-13 | **Chaos bounds the simulator** — `H* ≈ (1/λ)·ln(ε/|δ0|)`, **logarithmic in precision**; a simulator **must report the horizon** over which its output is meaningful; past H* it is compute spent on noise | VIII | ✅ **DONE** — `core/horizon.rs`, 12 tests. ★★★ **The horizon is part of the answer, not an annotation on it**: `Bounded<T>::read()` returns `Option`, so a reading past its own horizon has no value to take — a flag beside a number is one a caller can forget to check. ★★★ **λ is measured or it is `Unknown`, never assumed** — an assumed exponent yields a horizon that looks like a measurement, and one wrong in the generous direction licenses exactly the compute it should refuse; `Unknown` therefore `covers()` **nothing**. ★★★ **`Unbounded` is deliberately not called `Infinite`**: λ ≤ 0 says *chaos does not bound this*, and an unmodelled event or a changed rule still ends a forecast. ★★ **The logarithm made askable** — `steps_bought_by(factor, λ) = ln(factor)/λ`, independent of where you started, so "what if we measured ten times more precisely" is a number (three steps) somebody can look at before funding it. ★★ `ε ≤ δ₀` reports **zero steps**, a real answer — you cannot simulate this usefully at all — rather than a negative horizon. Unresolvable separations are skipped, not clamped to the floor, which would invent a growth rate from the floor. |
| OPV-14 | **Self-organised criticality** — `P(s)∝s^-τ`; `τ≤2 ⇒ E[s]` diverges, `τ≤3 ⇒ Var[s]` diverges ⇒ **"expected loss" is not a usable summary near criticality**; three detectors: **branching ratio σ̂ (a read over the log's `causes`)**, tail shape (Clauset MLE, not a log-log eyeball), **critical slowing down** (variance + lag-1 autocorr — the Monitor's own CUSUM/EWMA moments) | VIII | 🔨 **σ̂ + detector 3 built 2026-08-16** (`criticality.rs` · `branching_ratio`, `BranchingReading`, `CascadeSummary`, `Regime`, `CriticalitySpec`, `critical_slowing_down`); **tail shape deliberately ⬜, for a structural reason.** The dependency was **verified before building**: `causes` is on the Rust `Event` and `assign_clocks` derives the DAG from it, so σ̂ adds no field. ★★ **The risk-arithmetic point is a TYPE, not a caveat:** there is **no method that returns a bare expected cascade size** — `cascade_summary()` returns a `CascadeSummary` whose `Mean` variant is unreachable at or above criticality. Not a wider interval, not a caveated number — **no number**. And withheld **at** the band, not only above it: being inside it is precisely not knowing which side of `τ ≤ 2` you are on. The observed largest cascade is reported as a **FLOOR**, since §VIII says the bound is the system's, *"not history"*, and an observed maximum is exactly history. ★★ **A reading surfaces, never triggers** — structural, not conventional: everything takes `&[Event]`/`&[f64]`, so the borrow checker enforces that a reading cannot change what it read; no engine, no operator, no `&mut` in the module. ★ **Window edges handled where this estimate quietly goes wrong:** a cause outside the window is **dangling and reported**, never an offspring nobody had; a tree over a finite window is pinned as **never supercritical** (`n` nodes, `n−1` edges); the critical band is **declared** rather than a hidden epsilon, and the same tree reads critical at 0.2 and subcritical at 0.05 — the band is the judgement, and it is arguable. ★ **Tail shape is slotted for a STRUCTURAL reason, not scheduling:** CSN's goodness-of-fit step is a semi-parametric **bootstrap**, which needs a random source ADR-0001 forbids in core. The MLE and lognormal likelihood-ratio are closed-form and could ship — but without GoF there is no answer to *"is a power law even a plausible fit"*, and shipping the two computable thirds would license a `τ` that reads as a fitted power law and is not one. **The constraint a future slice must satisfy is already recorded: the verdict type may not carry an absolute "power law" variant, only a comparison.** Out of scope and named: the `⟨λ,β,γ⟩` weight modulation (§VIII's scoring *implication*, which the article marks as design not derivation) and `scenario_ensemble` (§VIII-iii, constructive chaos) |
| OPV-15 | **Scenario ensemble** — `vary` applied to **initial conditions**: sample declared perturbations, fork, simulate under the **same** admit, return the **Pareto front + H\***; the bridge from complicated-domain optimisation to **complex-domain probing** | VIII | ✅ **DONE** — `core/probe.rs`, 10 tests, over OPV-18's `Score` and OPV-13's `Horizon`. ★★★ **The front is returned WITH its horizon** — a front without one is a recommendation with the "for how long" removed. ★★★ **The disagreement is the finding and is never collapsed**: if one candidate wins on a good start and another on a bad one, `is_contested()` says so and `contested_between()` names which start prefers which. A single winner hides exactly that and looks more confident for having hidden it. ★★★ **CANON TRANSLATION, declared:** §VIII says *sample* declared perturbations; this core has no random source by construction, so the declared perturbations **are** the sample. Not a weakening — an enumerated set is reproducible, so a probe run twice gives the same front and "why did it recommend that in March" has an answer; a random sample would give a different front from the same inputs. Tested as a property. ★★ `Perturbation::as_measured()` is always one of the forks, or the probe answers a question about worlds nobody is in. ★★ Dominated candidates are reported **with who beat them** — an absence is not a finding. ★★ A candidate not run under every starting point is neither dominant nor dominated: it has not beaten anything there and has not lost. |
| OPV-16 | **Suited domains** `dom_i ⊆ {clear, complicated, complex, chaotic}`; `match(i,s)`, mismatch ⇒ **abstain**; **coverage condition** `⋃dom_i` must cover all four or **surface the gap** rather than route to the least-bad scorer; *disorder* is a detection target | IX | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`cynefin.rs` · `ResponseMode`, `DomainReading`, `Suitability`, `Mismatch`, `DomainCoverage`, `Disorder`, `DomainRouting`, `assess`, `domain_coverage`, `route_on_domain`, `transition_risk`) — **and this closes §XV's three duties.** ⚠ **The collision this row recorded in advance HELD**: `operatives.<n>.domain` is still the subject-matter tag list, the axis was named `Cynefin` when `ω` shipped, and both stayed expressible — a flag raised before either existed and honoured at the moment it would have been sprung. ★★ **This layer routes ON a domain reading and does NOT produce one.** §IX assigns `dom(s)` to the Monitor and gives **no** classification algorithm; inventing a confident one would be the confident-classification-from-nowhere refused everywhere else here (`0/0 ≠ r=0`; a missing cost is not free). So `DomainReading` is **supplied**, and the classifier is **CAP-13 / §4D.6 — a separate LAYER, not an agent-layer gap.** ★★ **What actually closed Route:** an **indeterminate** reading is now representable, so a caller with no reading surfaces disorder instead of **fabricating a regime** to call `route()` at all — that was the real agent-side gap, and naming it is what makes the `Built` flip honest rather than asserted. ★ **The abstention's reason is derived, not canned:** each response mode carries what it *presupposes*, so a `Mismatch` names the presupposition that fails against the regime that denies it — §IX's *"not merely less useful; confidently wrong, because its method presupposes analysability"* reachable from declared data. ★ **Disorder is UNDECLARABLE by construction**, not by rule: `Cynefin` has exactly four variants, so `dom_i = {disorder}` has no value to write. A `GoodhartGuard` **is** a `ConstraintDecl` and `check()` is a thin call to the **existing** `admit_one` — if it did anything the gate does not, the module would have re-invented what §XIV says to reuse. It carries **no utility, no vote, no score, no rank**: there is nothing on the type that could become another scoring voice. ★★ **Proof of value, two-part because the claim is two-part:** sleep falls 8 → 3 and **every operative reads the two states as identical** (`u_i(before) == u_i(after)`, Δu the zero vector) — so nothing in the agent layer could rank against it however many operatives are added — **and** the gate refuses it via the declared U-invariant, while admitting the healthy state. ★ **A guard that watches nothing unwatched is REFUSED** at authoring time: watching what an operative already scores is a redundant voice, not a guard, and calling it one would be false assurance about the exact blind spot §XIV is about. ★ **§XIV's reason for refusing the obvious fix is demonstrated in both directions**, not quoted: adding an operative for a `U` dimension really does move it to `O` and shrink `U` — *a benefit and not a solution* — and it is still non-empty; then `S` gains a dimension one step later and a fresh `U` appears. Limits recorded: `U` is only over **declared** dimensions (no report finds what the model does not contain), and support matching is **root-name granular** — coarse in the **safe** direction, since it can refuse a real guard (visible) rather than silently accept a fake one |
| OPV-28 | **Orchestrator = sparse mixture-of-experts gate** `top-k(softmax(α·relevance + η·Δû + ζ·match))`; sparsity matters because evaluating an operative forks state and is metered; **relevance (subject) and match (Cynefin) are different axes — conflating them makes one inexpressible** | XV | ✅ **built 2026-08-16** (`mixture.rs` · `Mixture`, `Dispatch`, `Ticket`, `Contender`, `GateWeights`, `EstimateRule`, `subject_relevance`, `coverage_gaps`) — **§XV's third duty, and the one that made Route reachable at all.** ★★ **Sparsity is STRUCTURAL, not a post-filter:** scoring all `N` then truncating is a ranked list, because the expensive work already happened. So the router **performs** the call — `Dispatch::run` applies the caller's function to the selected tickets and nothing else, `Ticket` has private fields minted only inside `route`, and no method hands one to an unselected operative or iterates the population with `f`. Proven with a counting closure: **entered 2 of 4**, the other two never entered. ★★ **The routing scalar is walled off from presentation.** `Δû` is a vector and the gate needs a scalar — exactly the collapse OPV-29 just stopped a surface doing. The permission is narrow and stated: routing chooses **whom to ask, never what to do**. The wall is structural (`Dispatch` carries **no `Alternative` and no frontier**, so nothing in it can reach `present()`) and **proven** — present before and after routing, frontier **byte-identical**, including the non-convex option a routing-style weighted sum would delete. The collapse rule is a named `EstimateRule` so which collapse ran is auditable. ★ **The two axes cannot substitute:** `subject_tags: BTreeSet<String>` and `dom_i: BTreeSet<Cynefin>` are different fields of different types — §XV's warning (and OPV-16's collision) unrepresentable to violate. `subject_relevance` **generalises `is_relevant` and agrees at both boundaries** (untagged request → 1.0, untagged operative → 0.0), so it is > 0 exactly when the hard test is true and soft in between. ★ **Coverage folded in because it is cheap and load-bearing:** when `dom(s)` is in no operative's `dom_i`, `route` returns **zero tickets and names the gap** — §XIII says routing to the least-bad scorer is *confidently wrong*, not degraded. A domain mismatch withholds under **its own reason**, never scored, kept distinct from losing the top-`k`. ★ **Borrowed for selection, deliberately not for combination:** Jacobs et al. compute `y = Σ g_i f_i`; averaging expert outputs is the §II collapse one layer on, so `g_i` is reported and never weights an answer — and **softmax is monotone**, so it does not choose, it normalises. **Slots named, not stubbed:** `dom(s)` is **supplied** (a Monitor output needing OPV-14), `Δû` is **supplied** (computing it means forking — the thing routing avoids), *disorder* detection is OPV-16, and **Shazeer's load balancing is NOT built** — it is a training loss and there is no training here; `never_reachable` is the honest adjacent read of the dead-expert problem it exists to prevent |
| OPV-29 | **Orchie's three duties** — **route** sparsely, **hold the whole** (domain reading + criticality signal), **present the frontier not the winner**; showing only the top-ranked option silently re-collapses the vector — "a presentation bug with the consequences of an architecture bug" | XV | 🔨 **presentation duty ✅ built 2026-08-16** (`presentation.rs` · `present`, `present_joint`, `Presented`, `CollapseRule`, `Collapsed`, `Duty`) — **route is OPV-28 and stays ⬜**. ★★ **`present()` returns 𝒫(X), and there is NO method on `Presented` that hands back a single id on its own terms.** Reaching one option requires `collapse()`, which (1) demands a **declared `CollapseRule`** — *"because it was first"* is a rule and has to be said out loud; (2) returns `hidden`, everything the frontier held and the collapse dropped, **non-empty whenever there was a genuine choice**; (3) **refuses `SolePoint` on a frontier of more than one**, so the one honest reason to collapse cannot be borrowed to justify a real deletion. ★ **`would_reinstate_the_collapse()` is the §XV diagnostic**, and the proof ties §XV back to §II by computation rather than assertion: the option **1001 weight vectors deleted last slice** is the option a single-winner surface hides here. Choosing a **dominated** option is refused (that is losing a trade-off, not resolving one); presenting **nothing** is refused rather than returning an empty frontier, which would read as *"there is no good choice"* when the truth is *"you gave me no choices"*. `present_joint` is §XV's **hold the whole** at population level — an option only one operative rates non-dominated is still a real choice. ★ **The three duties are recorded with what is NOT built:** `discharged_by_this_build()` reports Route → `NotBuilt` (naming OPV-28), HoldTheWhole → `Partial` (the Cynefin domain reading shipped with `ω`; §VIII's criticality signal is OPV-14), PresentTheFrontier → `Built`. A status read that states its absences; nothing is stubbed. Limits: **presentation is not ordering** (an order over non-dominated options is a preference about preferences, and inventing one would be the collapse wearing a different hat), and which of the single/joint surfaces to show is the caller's decision |
| OPV-30 | **The approval token INSIDE admit()** — `admit ⟺ §4E ∧ (effect_class=sandbox ∨ valid_token(approve(o, principal)))`; `τ=⟨hash(o,θ), principal, proposal_id, nonce, expiry⟩` — **bound to (o,θ)** (else it approves a *shape* of act), **single-use** (at-least-once delivery makes a replayable approval one you gave once and spent twice), **expiring with re-admission at commit** | XVI | ✅ **DONE in Rust** (`approval.rs`, 2026-08-12). `EffectClass::Live` **carries** the token, so *"live and unapproved" is not expressible*; the binding is kept **structurally** (operator + the real params, compared by equality) rather than as a digest, so there is **no collision surface**; expiry is re-checked at commit against a host-supplied `now` (the core has no clock); the nonce is spent **at commit only**, so a refusal never burns an approval. Defer + re-admission already ✅ (R2 #11). **Python unchanged — recorded as a rust-ahead divergence in `approval.json`** |
| OPV-31 | **The trace invariant** `∀ live e: ∃ e_sim ≺ e_vote ≺ e_approve ≺ e, τ(e_approve)=τ(e)` — **prefix-closed ⇒ a safety property** (Alpern–Schneider) ⇒ **in the enforceable class**, so it can be structural rather than promised; depth cannot be used to get around it (Corollary 2) | XVI | ✅ **DONE in Rust as STRUCTURE, not a runtime check** — `Simulated → Voted → approve()`, each stage **consuming the previous by value**; `ApprovalToken` has **private fields and no public constructor**, so the only route to one is the full chain. A refused sandbox run cannot advance, and only `PASSED` advances — **`DEFERRED` is never an auto-approval**, because a deadline passing is not a decision. Depth adds a conjunct rather than escaping one (Corollary 2): there is one gate, and this clause is inside it |

**Summary: the gate half was already ✅ and better than Python; as of 2026-08-16 the AGENT LAYER IS OPEN.** The gate half stands as it did — the sandbox cannot bypass the gate (structural, one path), privilege is enforced, defer+re-admission ships, and OPV-30/31's approval token closed the module's strongest single safety gap on 2026-08-12. **What is new is the foundation the rest of the agent layer hangs off.** `ω` is built with **no `S` and no `T` on it**, so §I's sharing constraint is unrepresentable to violate rather than checked; **Prop 1 is structural** (a move outside `T` is unconstructible, not rejected) and **Prop 2 is at the type level** (`reachable` is never passed a population, so adding an operative cannot change it — asserted as the pair: capability byte-identical, ranking genuinely different). And **`u_i` is a vector that stays one**: no method returns a scalar, `scalarise` exists only to demonstrate what it deletes, and the demonstration is real — a non-dominated option in a non-convex notch, kept by the frontier and selected by **none of 1001 weight vectors**. Two things were recorded rather than smoothed over: Nash's degenerate case is **at parity and correct** (§II's own verdict) but its disagreement point is named in three docstrings and subtracted in none, and it is not on a production decision path; and **Nash needs one scalar per party**, so it belongs *after* the frontier, not instead of it. **OPV-16's `domain` collision was live and is honoured** — the Cynefin axis is named `Cynefin`, so the reference's subject-matter tag list stays sayable. **And on 2026-08-16 the layer's BOUNDARY was built on both sides.** ★ **Outward (OPV-27):** the Goodhart guard, and the file layout is the argument — §XIV says the fix moves *out* of the agent layer, so it is its own module beside the gate, a `ConstraintDecl` with **no utility, no vote, no score**, whose `check()` is a thin call to the gate that already exists. The proof is two-part because the claim is: damage to `U` leaves **every** operative's utility vector byte-identical (Δu the zero vector — nothing in the agent layer *could* see it, however many operatives are added), and only the declared invariant refuses it. A guard watching nothing unwatched is refused at authoring time; and §XIV's reason for refusing *"just add an operative"* is demonstrated both ways — `U` shrinks and stays non-empty, then a new dimension refills it. ★ **Inward (OPV-29):** the presentation cannot silently undo what the vector bought. `present()` returns 𝒫(X) and **no method hands back one id on its own terms**; collapsing needs a **declared rule**, reports **what it hid**, and refuses to borrow *"there was only one"* when there were four. The proof ties §XV back to §II by computation: the option 1001 weight vectors deleted is the option a single-winner surface hides. **Still ⬜ in the agent layer:** `Π` the operator-DAG (OPV-4), `M_self`/`M_world` (OPV-11), attention (OPV-7), learning (OPV-6), suited-domain routing + the coverage condition (OPV-16), — **and §XV's three duties are now all at least partial.** ★ **Route (OPV-28) shipped the same day** it was named as the gap: a declared-weight top-`k` softmax gate whose **sparsity is structural** (the router performs the expensive call, so an unselected operative's path is unreachable rather than merely skipped — proven by a counting closure entering 2 of 4), with the **routing collapse walled off from presentation** and the wall *proven* rather than asserted (frontier byte-identical before and after routing). It surfaces a coverage gap instead of routing to the least-bad scorer, keeps *not suited* distinct from *out-scored*, and takes the mixture for **selection only** — averaging expert outputs would be the §II collapse one layer on. It is `Partial` rather than `Built` for one honest reason: `match(i,s)` needs `dom(s)`, which is **supplied** until OPV-14 feeds the Monitor's domain reading — **the same absence that keeps *hold the whole* partial**, so one row now closes two-thirds of a duty. ★★★ **MILESTONE — §XV's THREE DUTIES ARE CLOSED.** `discharged_by_this_build()` now reports **Route ✅ · HoldTheWhole ✅ · PresentTheFrontier ✅**, all `Built`. Orchie routes sparsely on a domain reading, holds the integrating view including the criticality signal, and presents the frontier rather than a winner — with a person choosing inside it.

**★★★ Update 2026-08-17 — OPV-4 shipped and the deep bundle is open** (`strategy.rs`). It is the bundle's one row **with a parity half** (`operative_graph.py` is Π in production), so the walk was measured rather than invented. ★★ STEP-0 corrected the brief in **OPV-1's favour**: `ω` has **no `Π` slot** to fill, because OPV-1 deliberately left none — *a declared-but-inert field reads as built* — so this row **adds** the mechanism and vindicates the restraint. ★★★ But the mechanism **Proposition 3 needs was already there**: `StrategyNode` holds a `LegalMove`, mintable only by `Shared`, so a node outside `T` is **unconstructible** — the proposition falls out of OPV-1's Prop-1 mechanism one level up rather than a second check beside it. The bound is then **proven, and strictly**: `reachable_under(Π) ⊂ Shared::reachable`, computed from `Shared`'s own transition table rather than a copy. ★★ Every node goes through `execute_admitted`, so `Π` has **no gate bypass** and a refused node stops the walk with the gate's own reason. ★★★ **And the reference's own graph does bypass it** — `run` calls `op_meta.fn` directly rather than `execute_operator` — which is a **shape** worth naming rather than an alarm, because the graph's output is an `OperativeProposal` and Slice 10's discipline is that *operatives advise, the human decides*. ★ The cycle guard **reports** rather than truncating, where the reference's silently stops. ★ **Residual worth carrying:** authorization and effects are passed `Unchecked`, and **a strategy is exactly the deputy IMM-7's capability was built for**.

**★★★ Update 2026-08-17 — OPV-7 followed, and it is the bundle's first genuinely reference-less row** (`attention.rs`). ★★★ The STEP-0 constraint that stood in for a parity grep: **narrow must not become a fifth notion of *important***. The salience family closed rival-importance on four surfaces, and a scoring attention is where a fifth could open — so `Score` has **no constructor**, is minted **only** by `narrow_scan` from `Region::distance`, and an operative has no field anywhere to declare a rival metric. ★★★ Both attentions are **structural** (two required fields, no `Option`) and a pair landing in one region is **refused** — the partition arriving through the back door. ★★ Narrow returns a `Score`, broad a `Reframing` with **no magnitude**, so the two cannot be weighed against each other; that collapse is what makes an agent a maximiser inside a frame it cannot notice is wrong. ★★★ The scan is a **path with fringes** and the geometric subtree is **unspellable** — 48 visits against 30,000 at `b=8,d=6`. ★ Depth walks the **real ⊕ tree** (`escalate` climbs it upward; attention descends), and the footprint reconciles with UI-1: *anything can scan widely and hold four; nothing can hold widely*. ★ Counterweight: the cheap-scan discipline is the **reference's own** (`should_evaluate`), honoured there by convention — this row made it a type, it did not discover it. ★ Residual: `ω` still has **no `attention` field**, for the same reason it had no `Π` slot.

**★★★ Update 2026-08-17 — OPV-11 lands, and STEP-0 changed what the row was** (`models.rs`). ★★★ `M_world`'s engine **already existed here**: `ensemble::ModelTemplate` + `Scenario` **are** §VII's declared-structure / learned-residual split, so `WorldModel` is a **framing** adding a fidelity claim and — the load-bearing part — an **identity**, which is what makes the correlation detectable at all. ★★★ The sharp question resolved structurally: `M_world` is `Σ̂`, an **authored copy** merely consistent with `Shared`, never a handle on it, and `agrees_with` is a **report** (`imagined`/`unmodelled`) rather than a guard — because §VII's whole claim is that the model is wrong *in ways it cannot notice*, and a guard would hide exactly that. ★★ `M_self` **composes** `u`/`Π`/`⟨narrow,broad⟩` rather than copying them, and `moves_within` proves **CELL's recursion** by construction. ★★★ **The keystone:** `Agreement::via_shared_model` **cannot yield `Independent`** — five councillors who forked one `M_world` and five independent observers are different *shapes*, so the masquerade is **unspellable**; and `BoundedAbove` carries **no estimate**, because the shortfall is unmeasurable from inside. ★ Counterweight: the reference's `weighted_yes = Σ confidence` is the named arithmetic, but it **forks per councillor first** and **never claims** assumption independence — unnamed, not misstated. ★ Residuals: `structure_is_declared()` is **always true** (the honesty, not a stub), the fidelity claim is **declared and never read by the gate**, and nothing wires either model onto `ω` yet.

**★★★ Update 2026-08-17 — OPV-6 CLOSES THE DEEP BUNDLE, AND M-OPV** (`learning.rs`). ★★★ STEP-0's sharp question answered by scoping: **the one place structure moves is the strategy library, not `M_world`**. OPV-11's `structure_is_declared()` stays `true` — a world model's causal structure is declared and only the residual is bound from data — while the memes, each a `Π`, are exactly what §IV's loop moves. *Learning changes the operative, not the world it believes in.* ★★★ **Proposition 3 survives variation by inheritance**: `vary` rebuilds through the same public constructors, so a node outside `T` remains unconstructible and an illegal `Extend` is a reported `MoveNotInT` rather than a dropped op. ★★ **`select` scores in the real gated sandbox** (`EffectClass::Sandbox` through `execute_admitted`) and returns a **Pareto frontier, never a winner** — collapsing `u_i` to pick one survivor would be an architecture bug here, since the survivor is what the library keeps. A gate refusal is **a bad score, not an error**; a missing dimension is **`Unscorable`, not zero**. ★★ **Results, not a clock, structurally**: `Feedback` has §IV's three variants and no `Scheduled`, `learn` requires one, and `from_projection` returns `None` when the projection agrees — *a step with no triggering result is a no-op, said by the constructor*. ★★★ **The attractor reading is instrumented and never asserted**: `proves_convergence()` is `false` for every value, the same device as `structure_is_declared` always being `true`; the trace answers *did it settle* and *did the survivors score better* without claiming either. ★★★ **NFL made structural**: a library is **scoped**, `Meme` has no public constructor, and `import` is the only door for a foreign strategy and **always** stamps its origin — a stranger's meme cannot be written down as native, and it arrives **without the donor's score**. ★★ **Counterweight, and it is real**: the reference runs this loop one layer down over **ParseRules**, append-only versioned and genuinely **feedback-triggered** — the discipline is its own practice, declared here rather than discovered. The finding is what it lacks: selection there is a **corpus regression check, not a fitness**, and there is **no NFL scope** (`provenance` is a settable string). ★ A real defect caught by a test: two `Optimise` ops on one node+param minted the **same variant id**, silently conflating two variants — fixed at the root, id by position, label by value. ★ **Residuals**: no crossover, `retain` is append-only (*forgetting is a separate decision from learning*), selection scores the end state only, no cost is charged for a round, and **nothing wires a library onto `ω` yet** — the fourth deliberate omission for the same stated reason.

★★ **And the route there is left visible, including a wrong prediction of this summary's own.** It previously said OPV-14 was *"the single highest-leverage row, since it moves both Route and HoldTheWhole to `Built`."* **It did not** — §XIII calls the criticality detector *"one of the inputs"* to a domain reading, not the classification — and a related string conflated `dom_i` (an operative's **suited** domains, which `ω` carries) with `dom(s)` (the Sustain's **current** regime). **OPV-16 closed them, and for a specific reason worth keeping:** an **indeterminate** `dom(s)` became representable, so a caller with no reading surfaces **disorder** instead of fabricating a regime to route at all. That was the real agent-side gap. **`dom(s)` is still supplied** — §IX assigns the classifier to the Monitor (CAP-13 / §4D.6), which is a separate **layer**, not an agent-layer gap, and the distinction is what makes `Built` honest rather than asserted. **Criticality (OPV-14) is ✅ for σ̂ and detector 3**, tail shape slotted structurally. One item stays *cheap and high-value once V is a region*: **runway** (OPV-19).

#### M-MUL · Multiparty  ✅ read
*Article: `3B1B — Multiparty - Formal Execution.md` (SLIME). Compose person-holons into shared wholes using only local information — and never erase the node.*

**Status: the convergence substrate is ✅ in Rust (event-time, dedupe, LWW join). The distributed-coordination layer above it — quorum, consensus, routing — is unbuilt everywhere.**

The clock is no longer load-bearing for the *answer*; it is still reported, because *why* two replicas differed stays worth knowing. **OR-set: add wins, and not by a tie-break** — a remove tombstones only the tags it *observed*, so a concurrent add was never what the remove was about. **RGA: the read is a pure function of the merged set**, so convergence of the set is convergence of the sequence; an element whose anchor has not arrived is **reported pending, never dropped and never placed by guess** (guessing would give two replicas holding the same set two different reads). Limits recorded: **no RNG in the crate** so tags/ids are caller-supplied `(node, seq)`; **tombstones are never collected** (a vanished tag could not suppress a retry, a vanished element would orphan a concurrent insert) and GC needs a causal-stability barrier this build does not have; and `converges` **refuses above 7 updates rather than sampling** — a convergence proof over a sample is not a proof |

Stability is *bought*, and the price is a number, not a claim. **★ `r` is DERIVED, not asserted:** `SharedInterest::from_rollup` normalises the composition roll-up's per-member contributions, so `Σr = 1` by construction — a member cannot get back more than the whole. Two boundaries fall out of the arithmetic and are §VII's actual content: a single-member body has `r = 1` so stability collapses to `b > c`, and the larger the body the smaller each share, so **cooperation is harder to sustain the more diluted your stake is**. **`rb > c` IS the ESS condition** for the deviation §VII names (payoff `r·b − c` against a defector's `0`), *proved rather than asserted* — with the honest limit that it assumes the defector is **pivotal**, which `ρ > 1` structurally makes them not (the free-rider tension, disclosed; resolving it needs the group's *response* to defection, which §VII does not specify). **Costly signalling made checkable** rather than noted: `signals_commitment()` is false for a free role — stable trivially, therefore evidence of nothing. | MUL-15 | **★ This paper is the canonical home of two primitives the core modules reference rather than redefine** — **(1) the Signal primitive (§III)**, a relayed refractory pulse needing no broadcaster, is the **shared** signal that **Monitor (§2), Controller (§3) and Tenet (§4) all ride**, and those papers cross-reference §III rather than restating it; **(2) the conductance-adaptive router (§VIII)** is Mycelium's flow-reinforced routing, which the Mycelium whitepaper **builds on**.
