The row's own argument held exactly as written: one primitive discharged a dependency in three modules, and the second half runs on the same declared topology the coordination layer already had |
| MUL-16 | **§VI's shared-CRDT roll-up IS §4D's person-first ρ** — the two are the same operation named from two sides, not a coincidence to be reconciled later. *(The §VI worked example is generalised — no household is named.)* | Add. | ✅ structural (= **MUL-11**; roll-up is a fold over members, parent never overwrites) |

**Summary: ✅ M-MUL IS COMPLETE — every row, end to end. The first article module fully finished.** Nine sittings, built from the substrate up. The *substrate* came first — Lamport ordering, the total order, dedupe, the LWW join, and the holon invariant as a structural property of the merge. **The Signal primitive (§III)** is the shared relayed refractory pulse **Monitor, Controller and Tenet all ride**, and its own argument held as written: TEN-8 was blocked on it and shipped on it the same day, and a fourth rider joined that was not on the list. **The Population (§I–§II)** gave a node a local neighbourhood and **no view of P**, with threshold commitment and a cascade whose phase transition is *observed* on the declared graph. **Consensus (§V)** made safety a property of the *body* — a quorum that need not overlap cannot be constructed — and **closed EDIT-8's `CouncilMint` loop**. **The Physarum router (§VIII)** completed MUL-15: a Kirchhoff solve plus a strictly local reinforcement rule, routing with no planner. **Vector clocks (§IV/§VI)** restored the biconditional the scalar clock cannot, and sharpened the LWW register at the point §VII flags against itself. **And disaggregation (§IX)** closed the bracket the Population opened: `disperse = 1[c < θ↓]` with a **hysteresis band** — eight readings across it produce two transitions where a single threshold produces five — and *departure removes an edge, never a node*, with `members_removed` asserted at 0 rather than described. Its settle runs through the merge the previous slice built, which is why the two belong in sequence. **And division of labour (§VII)** answered the question the whole coordination layer had been assuming away — *why a node would spend itself* — by making the answer **checkable**: `rb > c` is enforced as a refusal rather than printed as a warning (`Assignment` cannot be constructed on trust, and unstable pairings are filtered out of the search space before searching), `r` is **derived from the composition roll-up** rather than asserted, and the proof of value is a split that is genuinely cheaper, covered and redundant, fails only stability, is refused — with `price_of_stability()` reporting what refusing it cost. **Bounds and limits recorded rather than implied away throughout:** FLP means liveness is bought, never guaranteed; `k ≥ 3f+1` is computed but deliberately unmet; vector clocks cost `O(n)`; the router's *pruned* is a reading at a declared cutoff, not a deletion; and `rb > c` assumes the defector is **pivotal**, which `ρ > 1` structurally makes them not — the free-rider tension is disclosed rather than papered over, because resolving it needs a response to defection §VII does not specify. **And the CRDT family (§VI)** closed the last row and the module with it: the G-counter exactly as §VI writes it, plus the PN-counter that keeps its roll-up correspondence honest, the OR-set and the RGA — with the three laws executed rather than quoted (every permutation, every duplication, one state) and *never erase the node* turned from a property of how the fold happens to be written into a **theorem about `max`**. Its LUB also retires the choice the vclock slice had to make: a concurrent pair neither loses an edit nor comes back as two. **Bounds and limits recorded rather than implied away throughout:** FLP means liveness is bought, never guaranteed; `k ≥ 3f+1` is computed but deliberately unmet; vector clocks cost `O(n)`; the router's *pruned* is a reading at a declared cutoff, not a deletion; `rb > c` assumes a **pivotal** defector, which `ρ > 1` structurally makes them not; and CRDT tombstones are never collected, because GC needs a causal-stability barrier this build does not have. **Nothing is ⬜.** Multiparty no longer only *rides* the pieces that exist — **six modules now ride pieces of it**, and it is the first article whose every row is built, proven and recorded.

Two were shut: **brightness** by MON-7 (field-relative across the field, and `VisualSpec` has no public constructor, so a widget can only brighten by genuinely being further from `V`) and **score** by UI-1/UI-7 (urgency is the household's `d(s,V)`, cost is **derived** from the declared inputs, and the weights are the household's `SaliencePolicy`, which refuses to let urgency be argued into second place). ★★★ **The third — POSITION — had been claimed by no row, and checking it found a real lever.** The mechanism was two innocuous things meeting: `WidgetSet` is a **`BTreeMap` keyed by widget id**, `compose_view` additionally sorted candidates by `id`, and Rust's `sort_by` is **stable** — so wherever two widgets tied on score, the alphabetically-earlier **widget-authored** name took the higher slot. `aaa_spending` outranked `zzz_spending` at identical score. Ranks are competition-style (`0, 0, 2`), so a reader can see the third card is behind **two** widgets rather than behind one group. ★★ **And the residual array order among peers is the HOUSEHOLD'S declaration order** — `WidgetSet` now keeps the order it was loaded in for exactly this reason — so **renaming a widget changes neither its rank nor its position**, while the household *can* still reorder its own peers. **Summary:** the article's headline correction stands: **this module was specced as mostly-unbuilt and is mostly-built** — typed widgets, β keyed on event class, `compose(r)` as a pure computed value, a genuine knapsack, and deterministic effect-first capture all exist in Python. Nothing is in Rust yet (it is a surface module, correctly sequenced after the engine). **Update 2026-08-17 — UI-2 closed, and Rust now has a widget layer.** ★★ STEP-0's crux: there was **no load path in this core at all** (`curated` grep-0; every `widget`/`salience` hit prose in a doc comment), so the row became *build the typed load path with the check on it* rather than *port a checker to sit beside nothing* — which is also why it was cheap, since there was no untyped path to retrofit. ★★★ `LoadedWidget` has **private fields and no public constructor**, so an untypechecked widget cannot reach a surface; `inputs ⊆ dim(S) ∧ emits ⊆ T` is checked at load, and a widget's **own** declared `emits` again at emit — two nested containments. ★ `dim(S)`/`T` are read from `Definition` and paths from `predicate::parse_state_path`, so nothing is redefined; and a real typed `Edit` retiring a dimension makes a loaded set report **stale**. ★★ The counterweight is that **the judgment and the schema shape are the reference's own** — this core invented neither — so the gap was **one word wide**: *on*. ★★★ STEP-0 also caught MON-7's vector claiming a `curated_ui.rs` that does not exist; corrected there. **Remaining:** UI-1 and UI-3 … UI-15 in Rust. ★★ **UI-13 is discharged in advance by MON-7** (2026-08-17) — brightness is field-relative and `VisualSpec` has no constructor, so a widget can only look more urgent by genuinely being further from `V`. ★ And **UI-7's urgency blind spot is CLOSED — 2026-08-17**, and ★★ the finding is that it was **already discharged by UI-1**: there is no `spent/allocated` anywhere in the Rust urgency path, so no denominator to be undefined and no zero-allocation case to special-case wrongly. What genuinely remained was the *other* half of the row — the weights as *'a policy living in a module constant'* — now `SaliencePolicy`, declared by the **household** and refusing to let urgency be argued into second place; plus ★★★ `UrgencyBasis`, which tells an **honest** zero (inside `V`) apart from an **unmeasured** one (nothing declared), so a surface can say *silence* rather than showing calm. ~~It was no longer blocked**~~: it was blocked on M-MON because CUSUM/EWMA did not exist, and they have since shipped (`detect.rs`, MON-5/MON-6) — so the row is now buildable rather than waiting, and its `allocated ≤ 0 ⇒ 0.0` policy is a live decision rather than a deferred one.

★★★ **SURFACE-BLOCK RECONCILE, 2026-08-17 — every M-UI row is now in an honest state, and the sweep found one real residual.** Five rows were **discharged and never flipped**, and are flipped here with the code evidence rather than rebuilt: **UI-3** (β — `BindingTable`, keyed on an event class, and the ∪ as a sum type), **UI-4** (`compose(r)` — fresh per call, read-only by signature), **UI-6** (salience — and sharper, since urgency is `d(s,V)` and the weights are declared policy), **UI-8** (the budget — and Hick–Hyman **computed** rather than cited), **UI-9** (the knapsack — a genuine DP, proven against brute force). ★★★ **UI-5 was NOT discharged, and that is the finding.** UI-1's event-first eligibility withdraws an *event-bound* widget whose class did not fire — but a **Unit-bound** widget is always eligible, so one with nothing to show entered the ranking, scored `0.125` on relevance alone, and **was selected**. That is §III's *unexplained zero* exactly. Closed with a **grounding condition before ranking**: ungrounded widgets **withdraw** (not scored, not counted), the condition is **derived** from the inputs UI-2 already checked, and the withdrawal is **reported** — because an exclusion carries a score and a withdrawal carries a reason. ★★ **Two rows got an honest core-vs-app-layer call rather than a shrug.** **UI-10** is **app-layer**: its FSM state is a capture *conversation's* accumulated `known`, and this core is deliberately stateless across calls — a δ that exists to accumulate would be the first thing here that remembers between requests. **UI-11** is **app-layer for now with a named core slice**: the inference `ℐ : ε → (o, θ)` is genuinely portable (deterministic, no LLM, `θ` from the real signature, pockets matched against live state — every input is something this core holds), but it is fed by the **transducer** and by **capture history**, both app-layer, so a core `ℐ` today would be a helper with no caller — the *port a checker to sit beside nothing* mistake UI-2 refused. Queued for when a Rust-side caller exists, which is the same consumer EVT-15's un-wired `EnzymeCall` residual wants. ★ **UI-12 is deferred with the LLM/economy work** — and the **disposing** half is already built: a generated view would face exactly the same `WidgetSet::load` gate a hand-written one does, with no generation-specific path to widen. What is deferred is the proposer, not the constraint. ★ **UI-14 and UI-15 are honest notes**, confirmed: UI-14's two-widget-notions divergence is **Python-side** (this core has one typed notion, so it cannot be closed here), and UI-15 is a **coverage** fact — now sharper, since an honest *empty* view is distinguishable from one where cards withdrew. ★★★ **The M-UI block's true state: UI-1 … UI-9 and UI-13 ✅ in Rust; UI-10/UI-11 app-layer (UI-11 with a queued core slice); UI-12 deferred; UI-14/UI-15 notes. No unbuilt Rust-core mechanism remains in the surface block.**

Two were already shut: **brightness** by MON-7 (field-relative, `VisualSpec` has no public constructor) and **score** by UI-1/UI-7 (urgency is the household's `d(s,V)`, cost is derived from the declared inputs, the weights are the household's `SaliencePolicy`). ★★★ The third — **position** — had been claimed by no row, and checking it found a real lever: `WidgetSet` is a **`BTreeMap` keyed by widget id** and Rust's `sort_by` is **stable**, so a score tie fell through to alphabetical order over a **widget-authored** name. `aaa_spending` outranked `zzz_spending` at identical score. ★★ Closed by removing the lever rather than policing it: `rank_selection` orders on **score / urgency / cost**, none of them widget-settable, and past those the tie is **declared** (equal `rank`) rather than fabricated — the `Skew::Unknown` discipline applied to ordering. ★ The residual order among peers is the **household's declaration order**, so **renaming a widget moves nothing** while the household can still reorder its own peers. ★★★ **All three surfaces covered, and the property collapses to one sentence:** a widget can raise its prominence in exactly one way — **by the household genuinely being further from its viable region on a dimension the widget declared it reads.** ★ Residual: a surface must render `rank`, not the array index; the core reports the tie and cannot force a renderer to honour it.

#### M-EDIT · Editing engine  ✅ read
*Article: `3B1B — Editing - Formal Execution.md` (SLOPE). Change the definition while it runs — the meta-operator, one level above the Enzyme.*

**Status: the CENTRAL safety property is BUILT in Python — the migration predicate is real. The sharpest gap is that a definition edit is an in-place overwrite: there is no history, so there is nothing to roll back to.**

| # | Feature | § | Rust status |
|---|---|---|---|
| EDIT-1 | **The definition is a value** `D = ⟨schema, Inv, T_decl, …⟩` — editable, versionable, diffable; an edit is a **meta-operator** on D, subject to its own gate | I | ✅ **DONE in Rust** (2026-08-12) — `Definition` is a first-class value, and now genuinely **versionable and diffable**: `VersionDag` holds the append-only history and `Edit` is the typed diff. What remained of this row after EDIT-8 was the *versionable* half, and `version.rs` supplies it |
| EDIT-2 | **The typed edit taxonomy `e`** — AddDim / DropDim / RetypeDim / AddInv / DropInv / TightenInv / LoosenInv / AddEnzyme / … each with its own safety class | II | ✅ **DONE in Rust** (`Edit`, 2026-08-12) — the **structural / regional / transitional** families, each with its own `applicable(e,D)` guard and `apply()`. `ModifyOp` and the **peripheral** family are deliberately absent: guards and effects are Rust fns in this core, not data, and there is no utility/widget model — a variant whose typing obligation could not be discharged would be a stub wearing a type's clothes. **Python still has no `e` at all** |
| EDIT-3 | **The migration predicate** — an edit is safe iff **every live instance's actual current state** satisfies every candidate invariant; returns the **witness set** `{sustain_id, invariant_id, expression, reason}` | III | ✅ **DONE in Rust** (`safe()`) — witness set `{instance_id, invariant_id, expression, reason}`, matching the reference's own shape, **and since 2026-08-12 it judges `μ(sᵢ)` for a declared μ** (EDIT-12), which is what §III's *migratable* case actually needs. The `μ = id` half is **AT PARITY**, not rust-ahead — the reference genuinely implements it |
| EDIT-4 | **Fail-safe on an uncompilable candidate** — a candidate invariant that **fails to compile** is reported as a violation against **every** live instance rather than skipped | III | ✅ **DONE in Rust** — an uncompilable candidate invariant is reported as a violation, never skipped (fail-safe defaults at the meta-level too) |
| EDIT-5 | **Refusal vs error, in the return type** — a refusal is an *expected everyday outcome returned as data*; a `ValueError` is a programmer/input error and is *raised*. The Embroidery distinction (a fact about the household vs a fact about the document) honoured in the signature | III | ✅ **DONE in Rust, structurally** — the distinction is carried by *types*, not by raise-versus-return: a refusal is data (`EditAdmission.verdict`, `Rollback`, `Compatibility`) and a malformed declaration is a separate error type (`Irreversible`, `TransitionDeclError`, `MigrateError::ExpandIsNotAWeakening`). A fact about the household and a fact about the document cannot be confused because they do not share a type |
| EDIT-6 | **The safe-change asymmetry** — **loosening is free by proposition** (a weaker V cannot strand a state already inside a stronger one); tightening must be checked | IV | ✅ **DONE in Rust, and sharpened 2026-08-12** — `Edit::can_strand()` now names exactly three kinds (`AddInv`, `ModifyInv`, `RetypeDim`), because `Safe` evaluates the *candidate's invariants* against live states, so an edit strands only if it **adds or tightens an invariant** or changes what a value means. That precision is what makes EMC's `e₊` **auto-safe with no check at all** rather than merely cheap. **Python still pays the full `|inst| × |Inv|` scan on every edit** |
| EDIT-7 | **Versions, inverses and the algebra of rollback** — `D_n`, `e⁻¹`, and the **3-valued rollback** Total / Partial / Unavailable over an append-only definition DAG | V | ✅ **DONE in Rust** (`version.rs`, 2026-08-12) — **closes N3, the module's sharpest gap.** `VersionDag::commit` **appends**; nothing is overwritten, and `head` is a **pointer, not a counter** (two edits can branch from one parent, which an integer cannot express). Each node records the full `⟨D, parent, e, μ, author, t⟩`. `e⁻¹` is derived over the EDIT-8 taxonomy against the **parent** node, and round-trips are checked (`e⁻¹(e(D)) = D`). **Rollback is three-valued and `Total` has NO Δ field** — `Rollback::classify` is the only constructor, so *reporting Partial as Total* is unspellable, not merely guarded. Both pre-image escapes work: **folding the log** (free — the `AddDim` that introduced a dimension is already in the history, so μ⁻¹ is a lookup) and **explicit journalling** (paid). The algebra is a **groupoid**: `path_between` returns `None` for branches that never meet, and a path is no more restorable than its worst link |
| EDIT-8 | **Edit authority — the gate on the definition** — an edit is admitted through `admit()` with its own authority term, not checked inline | VI | ✅ **DONE in Rust** (`editing.rs`, 2026-08-12) — **the fifth duty on the §4E seam**. `admit_edit()` implements all four terms; **`EditEffect` has NO `Unchecked` variant** (the operator gate needed one for R1 parity, the meta-gate is new, so no bypass exists at all). §VI's *authority does not lift from below* is **structural**: `EditToken::mint` refuses a `Governance::Shared` definition from any single actor, and the only other route is a `CouncilMint` — private fields, constructible only from a **passed AND quorate** decision. **Quorum arithmetic was the declared M-MUL composition point — and it is CLOSED as of 2026-08-16**: `CouncilMint::from_consensus` takes a `consensus::Decision` (private fields, no public constructor, obtainable only from a ledger where acceptors genuinely accepted, drawn from a `Body` whose quorum size could not have been declared without satisfying `2q > k`), so a shared definition's rule-change quorum is now **derived, not asserted**. The `quorum_met` bool survives as one path among two, for a host whose consensus lives elsewhere |
| EDIT-9 | **The new definition governs immediately** — after a successful update, every live instance's spec cache is invalidated, because *"a version bump that doesn't reach the gate is a version bump that lies"* | VI | ✅ **structural in Rust — there is no cache to invalidate.** `VersionDag::current()` reads the head node, so a committed edit governs the next call by construction. Python needs `self._specs.pop(...)` per live instance precisely because it has a per-instance spec cache; the Rust core has none, so *"a version bump that doesn't reach the gate"* is not expressible |
| EDIT-10 | **Typing on the authoring path** — compile every invariant against the candidate schema and **raise on the first bad one**; reject unknown dimension types, duplicate dimension names, an `authority` outside {binding, advisory}, and an Enzyme name absent from the registry | VI | ✅ **whole-definition typing DONE in Rust** — `typecheck(D)` runs `bind()` over **every** invariant against the candidate schema, on `D'` entire, every time. Proven by the case that matters: **`RetireDim` invalidating an invariant that never mentioned the edit**. Guard/effect/widget obligations remain out of reach while operators are code rather than data |
| EDIT-11 | **Replay under `D′`, and the limit that blocks it** — patch replay is **D-independent by construction**, so it cannot answer "what would this history mean under the new definition"; needs the **semantic** reducer | VII | ✅ **done 2026-08-17** (`stranding.rs`) — ★★ **and the mechanism was finished before this slice opened, twice over; what was missing was the DECISION.** **(a)** The **state** half has shipped since 2026-08-12: `editing::safe` is `Safe(e,μ) ⟺ ∀i ∈ inst(D): μ(sᵢ) ∈ V_{D′}`, judging the **migrated** state, reporting a witness set naming instance AND rule AND reason, treating an uncompilable candidate invariant as a violation against every instance rather than skipping it, and wired into `admit_edit` as its fourth conjunct. **(b)** The **history** mechanism shipped hours earlier as EVT-15 (`semantic::replay_under`). **(c)** The rest of the engine — taxonomy, authority, version DAG, inverses, 3-valued rollback, μ + EMC — was already ✅. ★ So this row is narrow by construction, exactly as IMM-7's reconcile taught. ★★ **What was ACTUALLY missing, and it is two things:** nothing called `replay_under` from the edit path (the history question was answerable and unasked), and `admit_edit` had exactly **two** outcomes — admitted, or `WouldStrand` — with `Migration` an *input the caller had already chosen* rather than a *reported outcome*. Of §III's three classifications the engine could express *compatible* and *rejected* and had **no surface at all for the choice**. ★★ **The crux, proven:** `D′` adds `balance >= 500`; an instance recorded 300 then 700 and sits at **1000**. `safe` passes it; `replay_under` refuses call `c1`, because the balance was 300 when that call committed. Same instance, same `D′`, two correct answers to two different questions — kept in **separate lists**, never summed, because a stranded state wants a migration and a stranded history wants a policy decision. ★★ **And the distinction recurs in the REMEDIES, which is the stronger statement: migration cannot fix a history.** μ transforms where an instance *is* and cannot reach back, so `Remedy::Migrate` carries the whole history-stranded list as residue even when it clears the states completely. **Grandfathering is withheld while the state is stranded** — a soundness argument, not a policy: scoping `D′` to new actions cannot help an instance already sitting somewhere `D′` forbids, since the next call refuses anyway; there, `Refuse` is the only option, which is exactly what a silent default would have hidden. ★ **No default, structurally:** `remedies()` is the only accessor, returns a `Vec`, has no `best()`/`recommended()` and no ordering that reads as a ranking, and `Remedy` implements no `Default` — the OPV-29 frontier discipline applied to editing. **Residuals:** the remedies are **offered, not applied** (threading a chosen one back through `admit_edit`, and giving the engine somewhere durable to remember that a history was grandfathered, is host state and the sequenced next step); **grandfathering has no enforcement arm** here (per-instance effective-definition pointers are host state); **nothing verifies a supplied history is the instance's own** (EVT-15's *call log not wired to `execute`*, seen from the consumer side); and ★★ the history check **cannot see `μ` or `F`** — EVT-15's limit carries unchanged, because boundary and firewall are not part of a `Definition`. |
| EDIT-12 | **Expand–Migrate–Contract** — the three-phase safe schema change (add the new shape, move the data, remove the old) | VIII | ✅ **DONE in Rust** (`migrate.rs`, 2026-08-12) — **μ finally has a representation**, so the classification is no longer binary and the discipline is no longer *refuse-if-unsafe*. `Migration::Apply(Mu)` makes `Safe(e,μ)` judge **μ(sᵢ)** rather than `sᵢ`, which is the whole difference between refusing an edit and carrying the instances into it. **`Emc::run` is one call** — there is no public way to run the contract alone, so the ordering *is* the safety property. `e₊` is **auto-safe with no scan** (EDIT-6 refined: only `AddInv`/`ModifyInv`/`RetypeDim` can strand), `D†` is a **real recorded version** with both shapes, and `Safe(e₋,id)` is **verified** rather than trusted. Backward/forward compatibility are computed at **plan** time, so *"this commits you"* is told, not discovered. **Knock-on: a μ that journals its pre-image collapses EDIT-7's `Partial` region to `Total`** |
| EDIT-13 | **`π : D → G` — the definition as an editable graph** | IX | ✅ **done 2026-08-17** (`lens.rs`) — ★★ **and it is a LENS, not a projection.** `π` alone is easy; the difficulty is that it **forgets**, so the inverse cannot be a function of the graph alone. The editor is the pair `⟨get, put⟩` (Foster/Greenwald/Moore/Pierce/Schmitt 2007), and ★★ **`put` takes the old `D` as well as `G′` precisely so it can restore what `get` discarded** — a naive *regenerate D from G′* would destroy every detail the graph never carried. That asymmetry is the whole row. ★★ **STEP-0 corrected what `π` forgets:** §IX names *Enzyme bodies* first, and in this core it forgets them **for free** — `Definition.operators` is an allow-list of **names**, and the struct's own docstring already says *'operator bodies are not'* data here (they live in the `Registry`). So a round-trip proof over Enzyme bodies would have been theatre. ★ What `π` forgets that is **real here** is the **invariant expression text** (the graph draws *that there is a rule called `floor` touching `finances`*, never `finances >= 500`) and the **fine type detail** (`Number { lo, hi }` draws as the label `number`) — and those are what the preservation proof actually uses. ★★ **Both laws are executed, not asserted:** **GetPut** in the stronger form the return type allows — an untouched graph yields the **empty edit list**, not an edit that happens to be a no-op — and **PutGet** *literally*, `get(put(G′,D)) == G′`. ★ That literalness came from a deliberate design choice: the detail travels **beside** the graph in `Supplied` rather than as an optional field on a node, because `π` would always leave such a field `None` while an editor's node carried `Some`, and PutGet would have had to be weakened to *equal after clearing the fields π never fills* — a weaker claim wearing the same name. Keeping `G` purely structural also makes it exactly what a surface draws. ★★★ **The preservation, proven:** remove an operator on the graph, put it back, and the invariant's expression text and the dimension's `lo`/`hi` bounds return **byte-identical**, recovered from the old `D` the graph never carried them in. ★ And where the detail is genuinely absent — a **new** node — `put` **refuses and names what is missing** rather than inventing a plausible default; the one addition needing nothing supplied is an operator, because there `π` forgot nothing, which is the asymmetry made concrete. ★★ **A graph edit is a TYPED edit:** `put` returns `Vec<Edit>`, never a rebuilt definition, so §II's *an untyped JSON replacement has no kind* is honoured — proven end to end by a graph-added invariant becoming a typed `AddInv`, reaching `admit_edit`, and being **refused with `WouldStrand`** naming the instance and the rule (EDIT-11's own check firing on a graph edit), while the same edit against a compliant instance is admitted. A modified expression becomes one `ModifyInv` rather than a drop-and-add, which matters because `DropInv` cannot strand and `AddInv` can. ★ **Reuse over reinvention:** the edges are **derived from the parsed predicate** via `goodhart::referenced_dimensions`, so an edge is a fact about what a rule reads rather than a substring match, and the graph cannot disagree with the Goodhart guard about it. **Residuals:** this is the **lens, not the editor** — the interactive Edit zone is the surface block's work (UI-in-Rust), and what it gets from here is a real graph and a safe way back; there are **no operator→dimension edges**, because that needs an `EffectSummary` and MON-1 already found no shipped operator declares one (uniformly absent, so drawing them would be fabrication); an unparseable invariant appears as an **unattached node** rather than being hidden; and `put` does not reorder, since `Edit` has no move or rename kind. |

But it **permits editing `pawa_cost` and `license_tier` at runtime, free, with no authority check** — *"changing an Enzyme's price currently costs less than using it. That is §VI backwards"* — and it mutates the **in-memory registry**, so the change is process-local and lost on restart | Note | ⬜ (Python-side; recorded as a real defect) |
| EDIT-15 | **⚠ `edit.state_patch`'s `remove` sets the path to `None` rather than deleting** — under a typed schema that is **a type violation deposited into state** for something downstream to trip over | Note | ⬜ (Python-side; Rust's typed state + organisational closure would refuse the shape change) |

A defect in the definition becomes wrong ownership on real children, nothing refused, nothing logged | Note | ⬜ **a real bug sitting in the tree today**; two independent checks would each have caught it (typed overlay, typed edit taxonomy) |

**Summary:** **the ENGINE half of M-EDIT is complete as of 2026-08-12** — EDIT-1 through EDIT-10 and EDIT-12 are all ✅ in Rust. The arc, in order: the migration predicate was already real; **EDIT-8** made an edit a meta-operator through the gate; **EDIT-7** closed N3 by giving definitions an append-only history with declared inverses and three-valued rollback; **EDIT-12** gave `μ` a representation, so the engine can now *carry instances into* an edit rather than only refusing it. **Two rows genuinely remain.** ★★ **EDIT-11 (replay under `D′`) is UNBLOCKED as of 2026-08-17** — its own diagnosis was checked and **confirmed** (the patch fold really is `D`-independent by construction, and the ingredient really is in the Enzyme log — though in the reference's `operators_log`, not the Rust `Event`), and **EVT-15 built the reducer it named** (`semantic.rs`): `replay_under` re-runs the logged calls through the real gate under a chosen `Definition` and reports, per call, whether it would still have been admissible. ★ What remains is this module's own and is a **decision rather than a mechanism** — migrate the instance, grandfather the history, or refuse the edit — and it should be reconciled with `check_definition_edit_safety()`, which already asks the same question of live **states** where this asks it of **histories**; and **EDIT-13** (`π : D → G`) is a surface projection, not engine.

**Update 2026-08-17 — EDIT-11 closed, and the sentence above turned out to be the whole specification of the work.** ★★ It really was a decision rather than a mechanism: `editing::safe` (states, 2026-08-12) and `semantic::replay_under` (histories, EVT-15) both existed, so `stranding.rs` wires them and builds the surface neither had. ★★ **The crux is now a running test rather than a note in two vectors**: `D′` adds `balance >= 500`, the instance recorded 300 then 700 and sits at 1000 — `safe` passes it, the history check refuses call `c1`. Two correct answers to two different questions, kept in separate lists because they send an editor to different repairs. ★★ **The distinction recurs in the remedies, which is the finding worth carrying: migration cannot fix a history.** μ moves where an instance *is*; it cannot make a past call admissible, so a `Migrate` always reports the history residue rather than reading as *fixed*. And **grandfathering is withheld while the state itself is stranded** — soundness, not policy: the next call refuses regardless, so offering it would be offering a fix that cannot work. ★ **No remedy is recommended**, structurally — no `best()`, no ordering, no `Default` — because which one is right is a policy question about a particular household, and a library that picked one would be making that decision invisibly. ★★ **The counterweight belongs to the reference and it is strong**: `check_definition_edit_safety()` is fail-closed, runs before persistence, invalidates the per-instance spec cache on success, and its `blocked_by` already carries a real **witness set** — the reference had that discipline before Rust did. And it **names this exact gap about itself**: *'Only refuse is built... the safety check can only say no, not offer a guided fix.'* **Residuals:** the remedies are offered, not applied; grandfathering has no enforcement arm (per-instance effective-definition pointers are host state); nothing verifies a supplied history is the instance's own; and the history check cannot see `μ` or `F`. **With this, only EDIT-13 (`π : D → G`, a surface projection) remains — the ENGINE half of M-EDIT is complete with nothing outstanding.**

★★★ **Update 2026-08-17 — EDIT-13 closed, and with it THE WHOLE EDITING MODULE.** ★★ It turned out to be a **lens rather than a projection**: `π` forgets, so the way back cannot be a function of the graph alone, and `put` takes the old `D` precisely to restore what `get` discarded. ★★ STEP-0 corrected the brief's own framing — §IX names *Enzyme bodies* first and this core forgets them **for free**, because `Definition.operators` is a list of names and the bodies were never `D`'s; what `π` really forgets here is **expression text** and **fine type detail**, and those are what the preservation proof uses. ★★ **GetPut** holds in the stronger empty-edit-list form and **PutGet** holds *literally*, which came from putting the supplied detail **beside** the graph rather than in an optional node field — an optional field would have forced the law to be weakened to *equal after clearing what π never fills*. ★ A new node with no supplied detail is **refused, not defaulted**; an operator addition needs nothing, which is the forgetting made concrete. ★★ **And the graph edit is a typed edit all the way to the gate:** a graph-added invariant becomes `AddInv`, reaches `admit_edit`, and is refused with `WouldStrand` naming the instance and the rule — EDIT-11's check, firing on a change made by dragging a node. **`M-EDIT IS COMPLETE — every row.`** EDIT-14/15/16 remain recorded here as Python-side defects, not Rust build items. At each level the question is *can this level act?*; `Capacity::CannotAct` **requires a reason**, so a level cannot absorb a breach silently — the failure §V exists to prevent. **Reaching the root unresolved is an `EscalationOutcome`, not an `Err`**: the loudest thing the system can say must not be `?`-able into a log line. `Breach::from_ingested` returns `None` unless the reading really crossed, so an escalation cannot be manufactured. The observation handed to the acting level carries the **origin's** severity — a homestead decision about a soil sensor has to be about the sensor. `validate_holarchy` checks both clauses, with the `⊆` reading **disclosed as an interpretation** (§V never defines it). Adding the tier required **zero storage**, which is exactly what makes it composable here. ★★★ **Two tiers, and the persistent one is OUTSIDE the attention budget:** `view = persistent(ALWAYS) ∪ knapsack(dynamic, K≈4)`. **A panel that could be displaced by a busy day is not persistent, it is merely high-scoring** — so `View::spent` counts the dynamic tier alone, and the proof runs a budget of 1 fully occupied by a maximally-urgent widget with the panel present anyway; composing with and without it leaves `spent` and the selection byte-identical, so it **cost nothing and displaced nobody**. ★ It also shows when *nothing* is wrong, which is the point of `I(p)` being high for all states — *nothing is wrong* is itself the answer. ★★ It carries **no score and no rank**: ranking it would put it back into the competition it is exempt from. ★ **`WHEN_ACTIVE` needs no machinery** — such a panel **is** a widget, eligible through β and selected through the knapsack, so the policy is two-valued with a real behavioural difference rather than a label with one live branch. ★★ **`I(p)` is DECLARED, not computed.** `H(state | ·)` over a real state space is **intractable** in exactly the way MON-2's Kalman filter and MON-1's observability matrix are — both **declined imports** here — so there is deliberately **no `information_value()` returning a number**, and `PanelDecl::always` instead **requires a justification** and refuses without one. An exemption from the attention budget is the one claim that most needs to be written down to be argued with; same treatment `Region::weights` carries and `SaliencePolicy` gained in UI-7. ★★★ **Exemption is the FOURTH salience surface**, closed like UI-13's three by removing the lever: `WidgetDecl` has **no policy field**, so persistence is unspellable from the widget side — a `PanelDecl` is a wrapper only the household constructs, and the same declaration lands in whichever tier the **household** put it in. ★ **The divergence expectation was wrong, and in both directions:** `PANEL_POLICY` and `WHEN_ACTIVE` are **grep-0 on BOTH sides**. The reference has `PANELS` in `shell.jsx` — a hardcoded seven-entry **frontend nav list** for the Mycelium cockpit, with no ALWAYS/WHEN_ACTIVE distinction and no relation to Orchie's curated view. The panel *list* exists as navigation; the *policy* exists only in the article. **Residuals:** ★★ **flicker-stability is NOT this row** — keeping a *dynamic* widget in its slot across recompositions needs memory of the last view, which `compose_view` deliberately does not keep; separate work, named rather than smuggled in. ★ The canonical panel list (console / health / IoT / sessions) is the **household's**, not hardcoded here — a core shipping its own would be deciding what every household must always see. And a `WHEN_ACTIVE` panel is indistinguishable from a widget once composed, deliberately. |

| CTL-10 | **Control is metered** — worth = Lyapunov gain net of pawa; cheaper equal-gain intervention wins | Add. | ⏸️ economy-deferred |
| CTL-11 | **Frequency-domain stability (damping)** — Lyapunov descent alone permits an oscillating over-corrector; keep the loop damped (poles in the unit circle) so corrections settle, not ring | Add. | ✅ **done 2026-08-17** (`damping.rs`) — **and it closes Phase 2.** ★★ **The gap is not hypothetical, and the proof runs CTL-2's own checker.** Under `e_{k+1} = (1−g)·e_k`: at `1 < g < 2` the pole is inside the unit circle but negative, so `W` strictly decreases every step **while the state flips side every step** — it converges *and* rings; at `g = 2` the pole is exactly `−1`, so `W_{k+1} = W_k` and **Lyapunov's `≤` holds forever, with equality, while nothing settles**. A conformance case runs the **real `is_stable_intervention`** over both and asserts `stable` at every step. *Lyapunov says the gap closes; damping says it closes without oscillation.* ★★ **Honest fit, on MON-1's precedent.** *Poles in the unit circle* is a **linear** characterization needing a fixed gain. **Where a gain is declared** the pole is exact and is computed — `Damped` / `Deadbeat` / `Underdamped` / `Undamped` (with `sustained` marking the pole exactly on the circle) — because declining a real model would be as dishonest as fabricating an absent one. **Where there is none, nothing is fitted:** there is deliberately **no `fit_gain(trajectory)`**, since taking poles of a fitted model is precisely the fabricated-`A` trap MON-1 declined the observability matrix to avoid. ★ **The native form for the no-gain case** is ringing read from the trajectory: alternating-sign `ΔW` (`read_trajectory`) and repeated boundary crossings (`crossings`), with fewer than three points reading **`Indeterminate`** rather than damped — two points cannot show a reversal. And **overshoot** is the one-step form: 50 → 230 on a [100,200] band drops `W` from 50 to 30, so CTL-2 passes, and the state has crossed to the far side needing a correction back. ★★ **A finding from testing rather than design, and the reason both native readings ship:** `W` is a **magnitude**, so a loop flipping side every step while converging has a *monotonically falling* `W` — `read_trajectory` calls it `Damped` and is not wrong to. The **side** series is what carries the ringing there. Shipping only the magnitude reading would have made the underdamped case invisible **in exactly the way Lyapunov already makes it invisible**. ★ **Complement, not replacement** — CTL-2, `region.rs`'s `W`, and the EWMA series are reused, none rebuilt. **Slot:** the Additions' *Lyapunov gain net of pawa* is economy-adjacent and parked. |
| CTL-12 | **Potential + action** — `W(s)=d(s,V)` a potential well; viable path minimizes accumulated cost; Lyapunov descent = greedy one-step, **Tenet backward induction = the full optimal-control solution** | Add. | ✅ **closed 2026-08-16** — the potential well ships (`region.rs`) and is the Controller's Lyapunov function (CTL-2); **the accumulated-cost reading is now built too** (`tenet.rs::rewards_from_region`): with `R(s,a)=W(s)−E[W(s')]` the `H=1` sweep's `argmax J` **is** CTL-2's greedy `argmin W`, so the two are **one** optimal-control core. A vector computes the greedy choice independently and checks the sweep agrees — falsifiable, not asserted (= M-TEN TEN-12) |

**Summary:** **the Controller is now decision-math AND the loop that runs it** (2026-08-12, then 2026-08-16). The math came first: `is_stable_intervention` (CTL-2) defines a good decision against the **viable region** rather than an ad-hoc point; `AutomationTable` (CTL-4) is the declared *"how much does the human decide"* dial with a fail-safe default; `route()` (CTL-7) is the SNR filter with a Lyapunov-derived urgency; and **CTL-12's join is closed** — `rewards_from_region` makes `is_stable_intervention` and `backward_induct` **one** optimal-control core, checked by a vector. **CTL-3, the OODA machine, landed 2026-08-16 and is pure assembly** — it computes almost nothing, and that is the point: §III says Monitor owns OBSERVE, the Simulator owns ORIENT, and *"the Controller is specifically the DECIDE surface"*, so `OodaObservation` and `Orientation` are **constructible only from real Monitor and Tenet artifacts** and the machine cannot fabricate either. **The human seam is a property of what the type cannot do**: it parks at DECIDE, refuses to act while awaiting authorisation, requires a token bound to the surfaced act, and **has no method that mints one**. Boyd is encoded as a refusal — `decide` without an orientation is an error, so nobody is ever shown unscored options. A conformance vector walks the whole cycle end to end: **a signal fires at OBSERVE → Tenet's sweep ranks at ORIENT → the Controller surfaces and a human authorises at DECIDE → the gate commits at ACT → back to OBSERVE**, with the commit asserted rather than assumed. **One widening is disclosed rather than buried:** an action above the approval line executes under `EffectClass::Unchecked`, because the declared `AutomationTable` is the prior human decision — and the trace records `human_asked: false` so it is never invisible. **CTL-5 landed 2026-08-16 and completes the escalation half**: a violation at a nested sustain climbs the `MonitorEngine`'s parent chain — *"the escalation path IS the holarchy"*, so no escalation graph was introduced — asking each level whether it can act, until it reaches one that can (ultimately the level with a human at it). A level that declines **must say why**, and reaching the root unresolved is an outcome rather than an error, because it is the loudest thing the system can say. **Update 2026-08-17 — CTL-11 shipped and Phase 2 closes on it** (`damping.rs`). ★★ The row's substance is that **CTL-2 has a blind spot the article names**: under `e_{k+1} = (1−g)·e_k`, a gain in `(1,2)` makes `W` fall every step **while the state flips side every step**, and a gain of exactly 2 makes `W` *never change* — so `W(after) ≤ W(before)` holds for ever, with equality, and nothing settles. A conformance case runs **`is_stable_intervention` itself** over both trajectories and asserts `stable` at every step. Lyapunov is not wrong; it is answering a different question. ★★ **Honest fit on MON-1's precedent:** where a gain is **declared** the pole is exact and is computed; where there is none, **nothing is fitted** — there is deliberately no `fit_gain(trajectory)`, because taking poles of a fitted model is the fabricated-`A` trap. The native form reads ringing from the trajectory instead, with `Indeterminate` below three points. ★★ **And a finding from testing:** `W` is a magnitude, so a converging-but-ringing loop has a *monotonically falling* `W` — the magnitude reading calls it `Damped` and is not wrong to; the **side** series is what carries the ringing. Both native readings ship because each sees what the other cannot, and shipping only the magnitude one would have reproduced Lyapunov's own blind spot. ★ **A framing correction the greps forced, recorded rather than smoothed:** the obvious counterweight would be *the time-domain half is at parity*, and it is not — `lyapunov` is grep-0 on the reference side and its four `stable` hits are a stable sort, a stable fingerprint and two testables. CTL-2 is a **Rust** row; the real counterweight is **intra-Rust**. **★★★ M-CTL COMPLETE 2026-08-17** — CTL-8 was the last open row and it shipped as a real core property (the two-tier view, persistent panels outside the attention budget, `I(p)` declared rather than computed). **Row phasing settled 2026-08-17:** **CTL-6 relocated to a host-integration pass** (ADR-0001 keeps I/O out of the core, and the core-side half is already the ingest door) and **CTL-8 moved to Phase 3** as a surface row, on the same argument that moved MON-7. ~~**Still ⬜ in this module:** those two, in their new homes~~; CTL-9's assembled loop object is largely **discharged by CTL-3**.

★★★ **Update 2026-08-17 — CTL-8 shipped and M-CTL IS COMPLETE.** ★★ The row had been *moved* to Phase 3 as a surface row, and STEP-0 asked the honest follow-up: is its persistence **host state** or a **core property**? Core — and the reason is the sharp one: it needs **no stored state**. A persistent panel is not a remembered identity surviving a recomposition, it is an **unconditional inclusion by declared policy**, re-derived every call, so the tier fits a `compose` that deliberately keeps nothing and required zero storage to add. ★★★ **Two tiers**, with the persistent one **outside** the K≈4 budget — *a panel that could be displaced by a busy day is not persistent, it is merely high-scoring* — proven against a budget fully occupied by a maximally-urgent widget, and shown to cost nothing and displace nobody. ★ `WHEN_ACTIVE` needs no machinery, because such a panel simply **is** a widget. ★★ **`I(p)` is declared, not computed:** `H(state | ·)` is intractable in exactly the way Kalman (MON-2) and the observability matrix (MON-1) are, both declined here, so there is **no `information_value()`** and an `ALWAYS` panel must instead **argue** its case or be refused. ★★★ **And exemption is the fourth salience surface**, closed like UI-13's three: `WidgetDecl` has no policy field, so a widget cannot skip the budget by claiming persistence. ★ Divergence note worth keeping: `PANEL_POLICY` is **grep-0 on both sides** — the reference's `PANELS` is a frontend nav list, so the policy lives only in the article. **The remaining Controller items are in their new homes: CTL-6 in the host-integration pass, and nothing else is open.** **The spine reads: Monitor observes ✅, Tenet models ✅, Controller decides ✅** — wired into one cycle by CTL-3, fed by MON-9's engine, and as of CTL-5 that cycle reaches **every level of the holarchy**: a breach at a nano-sustain finds its way to whoever can act on it.

### Economy (deferred)

#### M-PAWA · Pawa & Juul economy  ✅ read  ⏸️
*Articles: `3B1B — Pawa - Formal Execution.md` (the meter, then the gas), `3B1B — Mycelium - Formal Execution.md` (the network as a Sustain), `3B1B — Arena - Formal Execution.md` (the market as a Sustain). **Held with the economy layer** per ADR-0001 D5, pending legal counsel — but captured in full, because the meter half is already live and the boundary between "buildable now" and "a later human-authorized act" is drawn in the article itself.*

**Status: 🔨 OPENED 2026-08-18. The METER (PAWA-1), the JUUL LEDGER (PAWA-2), the OUT-OF-PAWA GATE CLAUSE (PAWA-3), the RATIFIED ROYALTY FLOW (PAWA-5 + MYC-5), the TREASURY AS A SUSTAIN (PAWA-6) and GENESIS WITH MINTING MADE VISIBLE (PAWA-7 + MYC-6) are all ✅ IN RUST — measurement, balance, admission, contribution, the commons, and now the origin of the unit itself. GOVERNANCE (PAWA-11) is 🔨 — the mechanism is ✅ and `κ` plus the issuance rate are wired end to end, with the royalty schedule named as a follow-on. And ONGOING ISSUANCE (PAWA-8) is ✅ — the governed mint, **now wired into `execute_afforded` by the serving seam**, so a committed run really does pay its server. Still unbuilt: MULTI-HOST REWARD DISTRIBUTION (PAWA-9), and the market's settlement.**

★★★ **THE SEAM is where the economy stopped being a set of parts.** `Affordability::Metered` carries `serving: Option<(&Issuance, &str)>`, and one `if let` in the debit block — which already held the `PawaReading` — makes a committed run **charge the caller and issue to the server from the same reading**. ★★ That is what makes it structurally not a refund: the work that cost one principal is exactly the work that earned another, two different amounts on two different balances. ★★ Opt-in and **visible at the call site**, because opting in creates money and a defaulted helper would make the opt-out the thing you had to notice.

★★★ **PAWA-8 is the layer's second way to create juul, and the first that keeps applying.** Genesis answered *who may mint* with **nobody, afterwards**; issuance answers it with **whoever served the work, at a rate only governance can move** — and the two stay distinct in the entry itself (`MintAuthority::{Genesis, Issued}`), so *where did this juul come from* has two different, always-answerable answers. ★★ It is **denominated in work structurally**: `issue` takes a `PawaReading`, which cannot be constructed except by metering a committed run, so there is no `mint(amount)` and **no issuance without work**. ★★ And it needed **no new machinery** — one const, one accessor, one line in `declared_parameters()`. PAWA-11 built the mechanism; PAWA-8 is its first outside customer, which is the strongest evidence either row can offer.

★★★ **PAWA-11 is where a constant stopped being allowed to be the truth.** `κ` had been two module constants read directly by the meter and the gate; a governed parameter cannot also be a const that silently overrides it, so the constants were **demoted to genesis values** and **`compute_pawa` was deleted**. Pricing now goes through `Parameters::price` and the `&Parameters` in force is threaded through `meter`, `candidate_pawa` and the affordability conjunct — **one truth, enforced by deletion rather than by discipline.** ★★ And a parameter change is an ordinary Enzyme: **gated by the same approval token, bounded by ordinary invariants, recorded as an event, replayable through `replay_under`**. No governance engine was written.

★★★ **PAWA-7 is where conservation stopped being free, and the reconcile found the defect in OUR code.** Every earlier row's conservation claim held trivially because nothing minted — but PAWA-2's `credit` **raised circulation**, so it **was an undeclared mint**, which is precisely what MYC-6 forbids. It is gone: `Entry::Mint` is a **distinct variant that names its declaration**, `from_genesis` is a **constructor** (so genesis is one-time by shape, and *who may mint* is answered **nobody, afterwards**), and the accounting is exactly `circulation = Σ(mints) − Σ(costs)`. ★★ **And the word invites a confusion worth refusing explicitly: minting the INTERNAL unit is not issuing a real coin.** Genesis creates an accounting unit inside one host's ledger — no real value, no redeemable claim, no rail. The real-coin line is untouched.

★★★ **And PAWA-6 is the row that shows the layer is not a bolt-on**: the treasury needed **no new machinery**. It is a `Definition` plus a state; `V_T`'s reserve floor is an **ordinary invariant**; its Enzymes go through the **same gate**; its disbursements need the **same approval token**; and it **replays through `replay_under`**, which knows nothing about treasuries. *A treasury is governed exactly as a household is* — which is only sayable because it is built out of the same parts.

★★★ **The gas loop closes here, and it closes honestly.** `pawa::meter` measures a committed run; `JuulLedger::charge` debits **only** a real reading; and `execute_afforded`'s `balance ≥ pawa` conjunct refuses a run nobody can afford **before it costs anything** — priced against the **candidate's measured cost**, which the gate's own effect-on-a-copy makes free to compute, and never against the author's declared estimate. ★★ The whole loop is **opt-in**: `Affordability::Unmetered` makes the clause vacuous and every caller written before the economy behaves byte-for-byte as it did.

★★★ **The hard boundary, restated at the top of the module because every slice here is governed by it (ADR-0001 D5, and PAWA-13 states it):** the **software** — meter, ledger, gating, royalty settlement, treasury accounting, governance — is buildable now on a single host as **internal accounting**. **Issuing a real, public, transferable token is a distinct, later, human-authorised act and is out of scope.** Juul is a utility unit tracked internally: never real money, never a real transfer, never a payment rail — and the egress hard boundary holds alongside it. ★★ PAWA-1 is the easiest place in the layer to keep that promise and it keeps it structurally: **`pawa.rs` has no balance at all** — no debit, no credit, no ledger — and a test asserts that recording a reading changes nothing. ★★★ **And PAWA-2, the first module that DOES hold a balance, keeps it structurally too**: `juul.rs` has **no transfer** (a charge has no counterparty — spending removes juul from circulation rather than moving it), **no mint** (only `credit`, which *declares* an opening balance and is named apart from a debit), and **no rail**. The real-coin line is not merely out of scope — there is nothing in the module that could cross it. ★★★ **And PAWA-5, the first slice with a TRANSFER in it, could no longer keep the boundary by absence — so it keeps it three other ways**: a transfer moves juul **only between ledger balances**; `Entry::Transfer` carries **both ends in one value**, so `ΣΔ = 0` holds by shape and half a transfer is unspellable; and **there is still no juul→real path**, proven by reading the source rather than asserted. *An internal reallocation and a real payment differ here by the total non-existence of the second thing.*

##### Pawa — the meter and the coin

| # | Feature | § | Rust status |
|---|---|---|---|
| PAWA-1 | **`pawa(o,θ,s) = κ_c·compute + κ_s·storage`** — the meter of one Enzyme run; **metering, not estimating**: measure on real runs and record `⟨operator, ts, compute, storage, sustain, principal⟩` | 1 | ✅ **DONE IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`pawa.rs`) — the economy layer opens with the **odometer, not the gas pump**. ★★★ **The hard boundary first, because it is ADR-0001 D5 and this is the easiest place in the layer to keep it:** the software — meter, ledger, gating, royalty settlement, treasury accounting, governance — is buildable now as **internal accounting on a single host**; issuing a real, public, transferable token is a **distinct, later, human-authorised act, out of scope**. Juul is a utility unit tracked internally — never real money, never a real transfer, never a payment rail. **This module has no balance at all**: no debit, no credit, no ledger. A `PawaReading` is inert, and a test asserts recording one changes nothing. ★★★ **Parity with `pawa_meter.py`, term by term:** `pawa = κ_c·compute + κ_s·storage`; `compute = mutations + events + constraint_evals`; `storage` = **real bytes durably added** (the serialized event payloads plus the serialized mutations — the reference's own `len(payload_json) + len(mutations_json)`, asserted equal to that sum computed independently); `κ_c = 1.0`, `κ_s = 0.01`; and **wall-clock deliberately excluded** from the formula. ★ The `constraint_eval_count` distinction ports intact: an operator's declared pre/post-conditions **always** count, the sustain's invariants **only when the enforcement gate actually ran** — counting them otherwise would be *measuring a check that never happened*. ★★★ **And it is structurally stronger than the reference.** There, metering sits inside an `if result.succeeded:` branch — correct, and a **discipline somebody has to keep**. Here `PawaReading` has **no public constructor** and `meter()` is the only way to obtain one, returning `None` for anything that did not commit — so **a refusal meters nothing is a property of the type**: a reading for a run that did no work is *unconstructible*, not merely unreached. Asserted twice: `meter()` on a gate-refused run is `None`, and a `Meter` is **byte-identical** before and after an attempted refused run. ★★ **Reproducibility, which is the whole reason wall-clock is out:** an identical run meters an identical pawa (compute, storage and price all equal), and two readings differing **only** in `elapsed_ms` price identically — the exclusion made observable rather than merely intended. A reading with no host-supplied elapsed reports `None` rather than inventing a zero. ★ ADR-0001 makes it doubly true: the core has **no clock**, so `at` is host-supplied for the same reason `EffectClass::Live` carries a `now` — it cannot mint a time and therefore cannot price one. ★★ **The declared cost and the measured one stay DISTINCT, and both ride on the reading.** `compose::Enzyme::pawa_cost` / `OperatorMeta::pawa_cost` is the **author's static estimate** (composing by summation); the meter is the **measured reality**. Neither replaces the other — `PawaReading::declared()` carries the author's number beside the measurement, so where they disagree the gap is a readable finding; the test asserts a real case where the declared `0` was an underestimate. ★★ **κ is DECLARED AND EXPLICITLY UNCALIBRATED** — not fitted to any real infrastructure cost model. Storage being priced far more granularly than a compute unit is a **shape** argument, not a calibration. **No calibration was invented to make the row look finished** — the same honesty as OPV-7's `κ` and `Fidelity`'s declared-never-measured claim. ★ **Honest absence:** `Meter::stats_for` returns `None` for an operator that has never run, because **zero is a measurement** (a read-only operator can genuinely cost little) and *never measured* is not. **Residuals:** ★★ nothing here touches a balance (PAWA-2's ledger, then PAWA-3's gate clause); ★ the compute proxy is a **count, not a cost model** — one mutation and one constraint evaluation price identically, which is almost certainly wrong in reality and is exactly what a calibration would fix; ★★ `Meter` is **in-memory** (durable metering storage is the host's, as the event log is); and ★★ **nothing calls `meter()` from `execute_admitted` yet** — deliberately, since wiring it into the gate would make every existing call site meter whether its host wanted a meter or not, and the host owns durable recording anyway. |
| PAWA-2 | **Juul as the unit of account** — `balance ← balance − pawa`, admitted only if `balance ≥ pawa`; **a utility token by design**, defined by *use*, deliberately clear of investment-contract hallmarks (the gas-token posture, written into the design) | 2 | ✅ **DONE IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`juul.rs`) — the balance PAWA-1's readings are charged against. ★★★ **The hard boundary is STRUCTURAL here, not promised.** A juul balance is an internal accounting fact on a single host — never real money, never a **transferable asset**, never a payment rail. So this module has **no transfer** (there is no method moving juul between principals; a charge has **no counterparty** at all), **no mint** (nothing creates juul except `credit`, which *declares* an opening balance and is named apart from a debit — genesis is PAWA-7, issuance PAWA-8), and **no rail** (no I/O, by ADR-0001). | PAWA-3 | **The out-of-pawa gate clause** — `admit = guard ∧ constraints ∧ token_ok ∧ balance ≥ pawa` — one more conjunct on the existing seam | 2 | ✅ **DONE IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`execute_afforded` in `operator/mod.rs`, `Affordability` in `juul.rs`) — the seam gains its last conjunct: `admit = guard ∧ permitted ∧ result∈A ∧ D ∧ F ∧ closure ∧ token ∧ **balance ≥ pawa**`. ★★★ **PRICED AGAINST THE CANDIDATE'S REAL COST, NEVER THE DECLARED ESTIMATE — and the gate's own shape makes that free.** By the time the commit block is reached, the effect has already been applied to a copy and the candidate's **mutations and events exist** (they had to: `D` is evaluated over the before/after pair and `F` over the declared movements). So `candidate_pawa` prices the real run **at no extra work**, and returns **exactly the number the committed `PawaReading` will carry** — asserted equal, not assumed, because admitting on one price and charging another would be a real defect. ★★★ `meta.pawa_cost` is **not consulted**: pricing against it is the reference's own wrong path, where the estimate is `0` everywhere so the clause never fires. A dedicated test funds a principal with **half the real cost** while the declared estimate is `0` — an estimate-priced gate would admit; this one refuses. ★★ **Unaffordable ⇒ refuse and discard.** No commit, no state change, and **no juul spent**: the ledger is asserted **byte-identical** after a refused attempt, mutations and events empty, state untouched. *Refusal charges nothing* now joins *refusal meters nothing* rather than contradicting it. The refusal carries its own reason, **`insufficient_pawa`**, distinct from every other conjunct's — the same one-reason-per-conjunct discipline the gate already keeps. ★ Placed **before the token redemption**, so being turned back on cost leaves an approval **unspent** — the courtesy `execute_admitted`'s own comment already grants an invariant refusal. ★★ **Affordable ⇒ commit, then charge, exactly once**, against the real committed `PawaReading` rather than the pre-commit number (they are equal; the *ledger entry* is the committed one, so every debit traces to a run that actually happened).
