★★ `Affordability::Metered` borrows the ledger **mutably for the call**, so one call charges one ledger (concurrency is a host concern; this core has no threads); ★ the clause prices **the whole run, not per node** — a strategy walk charges each node separately, which is right, but means a walk cannot be refused up-front on its *total*; and ★★ **nothing credits anyone** — the debit still has no counterparty, so PAWA-3 changes nothing about the hard boundary. |
| PAWA-4 | **Pawa as a fitness term** `Pawa(π,s) = Σ pawa(o)` accumulated **in the sandbox, live state untouched**; `score(π) = Σ w·u(sim(π)) − λ·Pawa(π,s)` — efficiency ranked **before** commit | 3 | ⏸️ 🔨 the simulator accumulates per-branch pawa (Slice 15 step 2 ✅ app-layer); the **fitness term in a scorer ⬜** (= M-TEN) |
| PAWA-5 | **Contribution as a programmable flow** — `licence ∈ {Free, Royalty(rate, payee)}`; a run debits `pawa(o)` from the user **and** transfers `r` user→contributor; an optional declared **treasury slice** on royalty runs bridges the private economy and the public commons | 4 | ✅ **DONE IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`royalty.rs`) — `Licence ∈ {Free, Royalty{per_mille, payee}}`, and a royalty is **split across five recipients** per the ratified schedule and **transferred** between ledger balances. ★★★ **The FIRST slice in the layer with a transfer in it, so the hard boundary is restated structurally rather than inherited.** Three properties: (1) a transfer **only ever moves juul between `JuulLedger` balances** — an internal reallocation, never a settlement instruction to anything outside the process; (2) **`ΣΔ = 0`** — `Entry::Transfer` is **one entry carrying both ends**, so `circulation_delta` is `0.0` by construction and **half a transfer is unspellable** (a `TransferOut`/`TransferIn` pair would be two records a caller could write one of), and a **mint** — the only thing that would raise the total — is a **different variant that does not exist** (PAWA-7/PAWA-8); (3) ★★★ **the no-rail property stays**: there is **no code path from a transfer to real value** — no cash-out, no redemption, no external counterparty. **Juul is not redeemable, and that absence IS the distinction** — an internal reallocation and a real payment differ here by the *total non-existence* of the second thing, proven by reading the source (production code only, comments stripped, since documenting the boundary is not a breach of it). ★★ **Cost and royalty stay two different flows**: `pawa(o)` is a **cost** — no counterparty, and it **leaves circulation** (PAWA-3, untouched by this row); a royalty is a **transfer** — five counterparties, circulation **unchanged**. ★ `Free` transfers nothing and the ledger is asserted byte-identical: *free means free of royalty, never free of the work it causes*. ★ An unaffordable royalty is refused **wholesale** — paying the first recipients and running out would leave the split unconserved against its own schedule. **Residuals:** ★★ the **treasury is a named principal, not yet a Sustain** — `Σ_T` with its own viable region is PAWA-6, and the boundary is named rather than pre-empted; ★★ `settle` is **not wired into the gate** — which contribution a run is attributable to is the Arena's registry, not the operator's; ★ the **royalty base is supplied by the caller**; and ★★ **nothing here mints**. |
| PAWA-6 | **The treasury is itself a Sustain** `Σ_T` — `allocate`/`grant`/`disburse` Enzymes, a **reserve floor and category caps in `V_T`**, the protocol fee fraction φ_p, a periodic budget; **every disbursement rides the same enforcing gate and approval token** | 5 | ✅ **DONE IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`treasury.rs`) — and the row's own point is that it needed **no new machinery**. ★★★ **A treasury is a `Definition` plus a state, exactly as a household is.** `allocate` / `grant` / `disburse` are ordinary `OperatorMeta` values on the ordinary `Registry`; every one goes through the **same** `execute_admitted`; `V_T` is checked by the **same** enforcement gate; a disbursement needs the **same** `ApprovalToken`. There is **no treasury engine, no treasury branch in the gate, and nothing in `execute_admitted` that knows a treasury exists**. ★★ The recursion is demonstrated rather than argued: a treasury call replays through EVT-15's `replay_under`, which takes a `Definition` and knows nothing about treasuries. ★★ **The reserve floor is an ORDINARY INVARIANT** — literally `treasury.balance >= <floor>` in the definition's own list — so a breaching payout is refused with **`enforcement_gate`**, the *same reason and same shape* a household breach gives. **No floor-checking code was written.** Category caps are the same device. ★★★ **One truth for the juul.** The `JuulLedger` is authoritative; the Sustain's `treasury.balance` is **never independently mutated** — every outflow *requires* a `ledger_balance` param and writes `ledger_balance − amount`, so the state figure is a **same-call projection** that cannot drift across calls (asserted equal to the ledger after a real payout). ★★ And `pay_out` runs the gate **and** the transfer, performing the transfer **only if the gate admitted** — so there is no path that moves one without the other, and **no rollback to get wrong**. ★★★ **Outflows are conserved transfers, never mints** — circulation is unchanged, treasury + recipient equals the original total, and the treasury **cannot pay out more than it holds** (refused by its own guard *and* by the ledger — two guards, neither redundant: one keeps the state honest, the other the balance). ★ **A boundary named rather than pre-empted:** MYC-6's later distinction in which a *grant is a mint* is **not this row** — a minting grant would need an issuance policy nobody has declared. ★★ **Every disbursement is human-gated**: `EffectClass::Live`, so an `ApprovalToken` bound to that one act and **single-use**. A token for a different act is refused with `approval_token` and **nothing moves**; a replayed token is refused and the ledger is asserted identical to after the first legitimate payout — *at-least-once delivery must not become pay-twice*. An agent or strategy cannot disburse on its own, the same discipline that stops `Agent::act` firing a live effect. ★★★ **A real finding a test caught, not inspection:** a declared **category cap over a category the state does not hold refuses EVERYTHING** — `treasury.allocations.grants <= 500` against an empty `allocations` compares `None` to a number, a **type error**, so the gate turns back every operation including unrelated ones. Four tests failed at once. Fixed **at the root**: *a declared cap implies the category exists*, so `opening_state` takes the same cap list and seeds each at zero. Making the gate lenient would have been the wrong fix — a cap over a typo'd category would then **silently never fire**. **Residuals:** ★★ **nothing checks that the definition and the opening state agree** (UI-2's load-time typecheck is the real device, applied to a definition); ★★ the **periodic budget is a declared number, not a mechanism** — ADR-0001 gives this core no clock, and EVT-11's `Recurrence` is the primitive a host would drive it from; ★ **`φ_p` is declared and unconsumed** (what charges it is the Arena's settlement); ★ the treasury is **not yet composed** via `⊕`; and ★★ `pay_out` **takes the token as an argument** — the core cannot mint one, so who approves a disbursement is the host's question. |
| PAWA-7 | **Genesis** `g : principals → Juul` — the starting distribution, declared in the open once and auditable forever; the ledger begins with `g` and every later balance is a **fold of transfers over it** | 6.1 | ✅ **DONE IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`juul.rs` — `Genesis`, `GenesisId`, `Entry::Mint`, `from_genesis`, `Genesis::audit`), **with MYC-6 folded in**, since this is the layer's first mint. ★★★ **The reconcile that mattered: `credit` WAS an undeclared mint.** PAWA-2 shipped `JuulLedger::credit` and named it an *ungated declaration*; read against MYC-6 that is worse than ungated, because a credit **raised total circulation** — so it **was a mint**, and an undeclared one. **`credit` is gone.** `Entry::Credit` became **`Entry::Mint`**, which **names the declaration that authorised it**, and the only way to produce one is `from_genesis`. *Renaming it to what it actually was is the resolution*: there is now **no path anywhere that raises circulation silently**. ★★★ **One-time by SHAPE**: `from_genesis` is a **constructor**, and there is **no `mint` method at all** — you cannot mint into a ledger that already exists, so a second declaration makes a **second ledger**, not more juul in this one (asserted). *That* is why genesis needs no governance and could ship before PAWA-11: its answer to **who may mint is nobody, afterwards**. ★ `JuulLedger::new()` remains and gives a ledger with **no juul at all** — the honest empty case, not a shortcut around genesis. ★★ **Genesis is the ledger's base and everything since is the fold**: for every principal, `balance == g.allocation_for(p) + Σ(non-mint entries)`, asserted **exactly**, so **no juul is unexplained** — and `rebuild()` still agrees with `balance_of`. ★★ **Auditable forever**: `Genesis::audit` checks a ledger against the declaration and **names** what it finds (`foreign` / `mismatched` / `undeclared`) rather than returning a boolean. ★★ **The accounting, stated exactly**: `circulation = Σ(mints) − Σ(costs debited)`, transfers contributing **zero** — asserted through a real transfer, a real charge, and `Σ mints == genesis total`. ★★★ **And the hard boundary is restated where the word most invites confusion: minting the INTERNAL unit is not issuing a real coin.** Genesis brings the internal accounting unit into existence inside one host's ledger. It creates **no real value and no redeemable claim** — juul is still not redeemable, there is still no cash-out and no rail, and issuing a real, public, transferable token remains a **distinct, later, human-authorised act** (ADR-0001 D5, PAWA-13). **Residuals:** ★★ `GenesisId` has no public constructor, but a holder of a `Genesis` could clone its id and forge a `Mint` through `with_entries` — **closed by detection rather than construction**, which is exactly why `audit` names what it finds, and said so rather than overclaimed; ★★ **there is no burn** (if one is added it gets **its own variant**, for the same reason a mint does); ★ a **zero allocation is allowed** and produces a real `Mint` of `0.0`, because declaring somebody with nothing is a meaningful statement; and ★★ **nothing ties a genesis to a Sustain** yet. |
| PAWA-8 | **Monetary policy — issuance denominated in work** — new Juul enters by **rewarding hosts who serve pawa**; the schedule (fixed / decaying / target-rate) is a **declared, revisable parameter** changed only through governance | 6.2 | ✅ **DONE IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`issuance.rs`) — **the governed mint**, and the row PAWA-11 was built to make safe. ★★★ **TWO MINTING PATHS, AND THEY STAY DISTINCT.** PAWA-7's `from_genesis` is a **constructor** with no `mint` beside it — genesis is one-time *by shape*, its answer to *who may mint* being **nobody, afterwards**. Issuance is the **other** path: `JuulLedger::issue`, a method on an existing ledger, deliberately **not a second genesis** — a genesis is a fixed value you read once and audit forever, while issuance is a rule that keeps applying, so it needs revision machinery a seed does not want. The distinction is carried **in the entry**: `Entry::Mint` — still the one visible circulation-raiser, per MYC-6 — now names a **`MintAuthority`**, `Genesis(GenesisId)` or `Issued(IssuanceId)`, **neither of which has a public constructor**. ★★ **AND THE AUDIT HAD TO MOVE WITH IT — a real consequence, not a cosmetic one.** `Genesis::audit` treated any mint not naming this genesis as **foreign**; an issuance mint is legitimate, so calling it foreign would report a governed mint as a defect, and silently skipping it would leave juul **unaccounted for** and break the audit's whole promise. `Audit` gained an **`issued`** list: the honest statement is now *exactly this genesis, **plus** this much issued under these declarations*. ★★★ **DENOMINATED IN WORK, STRUCTURALLY.** `issue` takes a **`PawaReading`**, not an amount — the mirror of `charge(&PawaReading)`, and for the same reason: `PawaReading` has **no public constructor**, so the only source is metering a run that **committed**. **There is no `mint(amount)` on the ledger**, so there is **no issuance without work behind it**. `issued = rate × pawa_served`. ★ And the **caller pays while the server earns** — the reading's principal is *who ran the operator*, the server is *whose machine did the compute* — so an issuance is **not a refund**, asserted as two different numbers on two different balances. ★★★ **THE RATE IS GOVERNED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE SAFETY PROPERTY.** `issuance_rate` is a PAWA-11 parameter; `Schedule::Fixed` carries **no rate of its own**, so an issuance can only be priced from `Parameters` — the same one-truth discipline that deleted `compute_pawa`. Proven end to end: the rate is switched on by a **real** `governance.set_parameter` through the **real** gate under a **real** token, the parameters are **read back out of the resulting state**, and the issuance mints at that value and **not** at genesis's. An untokened change is refused with `approval_token` **and the refusal is asserted through to the issued amount**, not merely at the gate; an over-ceiling rate is refused with `enforcement_gate`. ★★ **THE SCHEDULE SHAPE, RECONCILED HONESTLY.** The **rate** fits `ParameterSpec`'s one-scalar shape exactly, so it is governed. The **type** does not, and is declared at the spec: a **decaying** schedule needs an epoch or elapsed time to decay against and this core has **no clock** (ADR-0001); a **target-rate** schedule needs the rate *derived* from circulation against a target, so the governed value would no longer be the rate at all. So `Schedule` has **one variant, deliberately** — three with two unimplemented would be **declared-but-inert, which reads as built**. Fixed-rate ships whole; decaying and target-rate are named follow-ons *with their reason*. ★★ **ISSUANCE IS OFF AT GENESIS** (`rate = 0.0`) — a statement, not a placeholder: an economy should not begin inflating because nobody chose a rate, and a zero rate issues nothing and **appends nothing** (a zero mint would be a log line that raised circulation by nothing). ★★ **THE EXTENDED ACCOUNTING**, asserted as an equation over a real genesis + issuance + charge + transfer: `circulation = Σ(genesis mints) + Σ(issuance mints) − Σ(costs debited)`, transfers at **zero** — and `minted_total − issued_total == genesis.total()`, so the two mint terms are **separable**, not merely summing right. ★★★ **AND THE HARD BOUNDARY, RESTATED WHERE THE WORD MOST INVITES CONFUSION** (ADR-0001 D5, PAWA-13): issuance mints the **internal accounting unit** — no real value, no redeemable claim, no cash-out, **no rail** — re-proven by a source grep over `issuance.rs` and `juul.rs` production code. ★ **Divergence: grep-0.** `issuance|monetary|inflation|mint|emission` across the reference returns **one hit**, a false positive (`pawa.py`'s *"bulk transaction issuance from the SQLite log"*, meaning batch writes). ★★ What the reference *does* have is the cautionary case: **`ONBOARDING_GRANT = 100`, declared and read by nothing** — and had it been wired it would go through `credit`, an **undeclared mint** at an unbounded, source-edit-only amount: exactly the Sybil-profitable inflation MYC-6 named, and exactly why this row was sequenced **after** PAWA-11. ★★★ **Counterweight — this row composes rather than invents**: PAWA-1's meter supplies the measured work, PAWA-7's `Entry::Mint` the visible raiser, PAWA-11's governance the revision path. **No new gate, no new Enzyme, no new engine** — adding a governed parameter was one const, one accessor and one line, which is the strongest evidence for both rows at once. ★★★ **ADDENDUM — THE SEAM (same day): `issue` IS NOW CALLED FROM THE CORE**, closing this row's sharpest residual. `Affordability::Metered` gained **`serving: Option<(&Issuance, &str)>`**, and the seam is **one `if let` inside the block that already existed** — the debit block, which already holds the very `PawaReading` issuance needs. ★★★ **ONE READING, TWO FLOWS**: the caller is **charged** `pawa` and the server is **issued** `rate × pawa_served` **from the same reading**, so the work that cost the one is exactly the work that earned the other — demonstrably **not a refund**, asserted as two different amounts on two different balances with the cost read off the **real debit entry** rather than reconstructed by subtraction. ★★ **Opt-in and visible at the call site**: `serving` is an `Option` a caller must spell out, never a defaulted helper — *opting in creates money*, so it belongs where it happens, and a constructor filling it in would make the **opt-out** the thing you had to notice (the same reason `Unmetered`/`Unchecked` are spelled out). `Affordability::serving()` is kept distinct from `metered()` because metering makes a run **cost** and serving makes it **earn** — different decisions about different principals. ★ Backward compatibility **measured, not stated**: with `serving: None` a committed metered run appends **exactly one** entry, the debit, and `issued_total()` stays zero; a **refused** run reaches neither flow, reusing PAWA-2's *`meter` returns `None` for anything uncommitted* rather than re-arguing it; and a **zero governed rate** issues nothing even when opted in, because opting in is **permission, not creation**. ★ Conservation re-asserted over a **live** path (three real gated serving executions plus a royalty transfer), within a stated float tolerance — the identity is exact in the reals and `f64` summation is not associative, so the slack is about the representation, not the claim. **Residuals:** ★★ **one server** (the core cannot know which machine served a run — no I/O, by ADR-0001 — so `server` is host-supplied like `at`; splitting a reward, and *verifying* a serving claim, is PAWA-9); ★★ **one schedule shape**; ★ **the seam is OPT-IN**, so a host passing `serving: None` still has an economy that only shrinks — now a **host's choice** rather than a missing mechanism, which is the difference the addendum closes; ★ **no burn**; and ★★ **`Issuance` itself is ungoverned** — the *rate* is governed, *which declarations exist* is not, closed by audit rather than construction, the same residual PAWA-7's forgeable `Mint` has and named the same way. |
| PAWA-9 | **Many hosts, one meter** — the decentralized network needs no new machinery: **Multiparty** carries membership and consensus, **Events-&-Time merge semantics** carry cross-host state. *"A global meter over a coordinated membership with well-defined merge is the whole of what a world computer needs at this layer."* | 6.3 | ⏸️ 🔨 **both halves are now ✅ in Rust as of 2026-08-16, and the row's own claim is what closed them.** Merge semantics: R2 #17/#18 (event-time, dedupe, LWW convergence) plus the **wider CRDT family** (MUL-10 — G-counter/PN-counter/OR-set/RGA, strong eventual consistency proven over every permutation). Membership + consensus: **M-MUL is complete** (Population, quorum/Paxos, vector clocks, composition). So *"a global meter over a coordinated membership with well-defined merge"* now has every named part built — what remains is **host wiring, not machinery**: the reference engine is still a single-process singleton over one SQLite file, which is the thing a second host actually changes |
| PAWA-10 | **Incentive alignment + security from the same meter** — out-of-pawa gating stops runaway work at the gate; **pricing-via-processing makes Sybil strategies uneconomic** (Dwork–Naor); the efficiency term rewards lean strategies | 6.4 | ⏸️ ⬜ not started |
| PAWA-11 | **Governance — the parameters are declared Sustains** — κ_c, κ_s, φ_p, φ_r, fee rates, the issuance schedule, the treasury budget: **a parameter change is an Enzyme**, admitted through `admit` with its approval token, recorded, auditable by replay | 6.5 | 🔨 **MECHANISM ✅ IN RUST 2026-08-18, COVERAGE HONESTLY PARTIAL** (`governance.rs`). ★★★ **Same template as PAWA-6's treasury, and the second time this build can make the no-bespoke-engine claim checkably**: the parameters are a `Definition` plus a state; `governance.set_parameter` is an ordinary `OperatorMeta` on the ordinary `Registry`; `V_gov`'s bounds are **ordinary invariants**; a change needs the **same** `ApprovalToken`; and it **replays through `replay_under`**, which knows nothing about governance. **No governance engine was written.** ★★★ **THE CONST RECONCILE, AND WHAT IT FORCED.** `κ_c`/`κ_s` were module constants in `pawa.rs` — *the* value, read by `compute_pawa`, which `meter`, `candidate_pawa` and PAWA-3's affordability conjunct all called. A governed parameter cannot **also** be a hardcoded const that silently overrides it, so the constants were **demoted** to `Parameters::GENESIS_KAPPA_*` — *the value κ is declared with at genesis*, not *the value κ has* — and **`pawa::compute_pawa` was REMOVED OUTRIGHT**, not deprecated and not kept for convenience. Pricing goes through `Parameters::price`, and `meter` / `candidate_pawa` / `Affordability::Metered` all carry the `&Parameters` in force. **There is no path left that prices from a constant** — one truth, enforced by deletion. ★★ **A change is gated, recorded and replayable**: an untokened change is refused with `approval_token` (a parameter change is a **deliberate act**, not something an agent does alone); a valid one commits and appends one `event.governance.parameter_changed` carrying **both** the old and the new value, so the event alone says what moved; and three replayed changes reconstruct the parameter's whole history. ★ **Bounds are ordinary invariants** — out of range is refused with **`enforcement_gate`**, the same reason a household breach gives; a parameter nobody declared **cannot be invented** (else `V_gov` could be sidestepped by naming something new); and a spec whose genesis sits outside its own range is refused **at declaration**. ★★ **Coverage, stated rather than implied — this is why the row is 🔨 and not ✅.** **Governed now, end to end:** `κ_c` and `κ_s`. **Named as follow-on, with the reason:** the ratified royalty schedule stays a constant, because governing it needs a bound this row does not have — **the five shares must sum to 100** — and MYC-5's conservation theorem depends on exactly that. `ParameterSpec` bounds **one scalar**, so a five-share schedule with a joint constraint genuinely does not fit the shape built here: a follow-on, not an oversight. ★ The treasury's `φ_p`/`period_budget` were already declared in `Σ_T`'s own state (PAWA-6), so they need nothing here. ★ **Divergence:** the reference's parameters are **module constants** — `KAPPA_COMPUTE`/`KAPPA_STORAGE` in `pawa_meter.py`, the royalty percentages hardcoded inside `charge`. Changing one there is a **source edit**: unbounded, unrecorded, unattributable, invisible to any audit. **At parity**: the parameters exist and are declared with an honest *not calibrated* docstring — kept verbatim, because making a number revisable does not make it right. **Residuals:** ★★ only two parameters governed; ★★ a parameter is a **scalar**; ★ **nothing connects a change to a council** (who may approve is the host's, as for a treasury disbursement — PAWA-9/10 are their own rows); ★ **no change takes effect at a future time** (no clock, by ADR-0001); and ★★ `Parameters::read` **falls back to genesis** for an absent value — deliberate (an ungoverned deployment runs the declared defaults) but it means a **typo'd name reads as the default** rather than failing loudly; the `parameter_declared` refusal covers the write path, the read path is the softer half. |
| PAWA-12 | **The migration path — single host outward** — (1) single host, (2) federated, (3) open. *"The meter, the unit of account, the efficiency term and the royalty flow are defined once, on a single host, and extend outward without redefinition."* | 6.6 | ⏸️ ⬜ (the path is the plan, and step 1's meter is done) |
| PAWA-13 | **★ The honest line, stated once** — the **software** (meter, ledger, treasury accounting, licence/royalty settlement, out-of-pawa gating, parameter governance) is buildable now on a single host, before any coin is public. **Issuing a real, public, transferable token is a distinct, later, human-authorized act with legal and financial weight** | 6.7 | ⏸️ **this line is exactly ADR-0001 D5's boundary**, written by the article itself |

| MYC-5 | **Treasury and settlement — the ratified two-revenue-type royalty schedule (2026-08-04).** A **pawa charge (usage)** splits `70 / 15 / 5 / 5 / 5` and a **licence sale (access)** splits `80 / 10 / 3 / 2 / 5` across contributor / treasury / validator / proposer / referrer. **Integer-floor arithmetic with the remainder assigned to the validator share**, so the split is **exactly conserving by construction** — a rounding leak is a conservation-invariant violation (of `V_arena`'s fourth conjunct), not a cosmetic bug. The schedule adds the **proposer** share and **separates the two revenue types**, which the earlier single four-way split could not express. Read the 70% as what it is economically: **the price the commons pays for variation** — selection (§VI) can only act on variation that exists | Myc §VI · Are §IV · Pawa *Add.* | ⏸️ 🔨 **the shipped `PawaLedger.charge` still runs the earlier four-way `70/20/5/5`** and has **zero callers** — the articles now all carry the ratified schedule; migrating the code is an explicit **Track-3 migration**, and the conservation constraint (shares sum to 100, remainder deterministic) is what makes it safe (= backlog E1/E2) ★★★ **THE RATIFIED SCHEDULE SHIPPED IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`royalty.rs`, with PAWA-5) — and it **supersedes** the four-way rather than porting it. Five recipients (**contributor / treasury / validator / proposer / referrer**) and **two revenue types**: usage `70/15/5/5/5`, access `80/10/3/2/5`. ★★★ **Conservation is a THEOREM, not a check bolted on**: integer-floor arithmetic with **the remainder assigned to the validator share**, so `Σ shares == amount` **exactly, by construction** — the leftover is not discarded, *it is where the last share comes from*. Tested adversarially (1, 2, 3, 7, 11, 97, 997, 7919, 65537, 999983, under both schedules). ★★★ **And the case the reference has no answer for is handled**: a single-host run has **no validator**, and often no proposer or referrer — **an absent role's share is folded into the treasury**, never dropped, because dropping one would break conservation silently. The redirection happens *after* the remainder is assigned, so it is a reallocation among the same five buckets and the sum is untouched; a dedicated test runs every optional role absent and still asserts exactness. ★ **Why the arithmetic is integral**: exact conservation over floats is not achievable and claiming it would be false, so the split is computed in `u64` — where the theorem is true — and the integer-valued shares enter the `f64` ledger. ★ The Track-3 migration this row named is done on the Rust side; the Python `PawaLedger.charge()` still carries the old four-way and still has **zero callers**. |
| MYC-6 | **★ Grants are mints, and the distinction is not pedantic** — a **transfer** (Σ Δ = 0) is bounded by the treasury and self-limiting; a **mint** (Σ Δ = +100) is unbounded, inflates the unit, and **makes Sybil registration directly profitable**. Both are legitimate designs; **only one of them is legitimate *undeclared*** | VI | ⏸️ ⬜ (the Onboarding Grant's kind must be declared before it ships) ★★★ **DECLARED IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`juul.rs`, folded into PAWA-7 since that is the layer's first mint). **`Entry::Mint` is a distinct variant from `Transfer` / `Debit`**, so a mint is **visible as a mint** and **names the declaration that authorised it** — *where did this juul come from* stays answerable forever, rather than a balance simply being larger than it was. ★★★ **And the row's defect was found in our own code, not only the reference's**: PAWA-2's `credit` raised circulation, so it **was an undeclared mint**; it has been removed. ★★ **The distinction is the row's own sentence, made structural**: a transfer is bounded by the payer and self-limiting (`circulation_delta` is `0.0` by shape); a mint is **unbounded** and is the only thing that raises the total. *Both are legitimate designs; only one of them is legitimate undeclared.* ★ **The reference's own case, named**: `ONBOARDING_GRANT = 100` is a declared constant with **zero callers** whose **kind is unstated** — nothing says whether granting 100 pawa per signup **mints** (raising supply per signup, exactly the Sybil-profitable case) or **transfers** from a treasury. Here that ambiguity is **not expressible**: a mint cannot be written as a credit. |

The no-special-power rule is a **structural claim** (complete mediation), not a governance promise — *"a mother tree with override privileges would not be a forest; it would be a plantation"* | X·XI | ⏸️ ⬜ (the structural claim is already true in Rust: one path, no bypass) |

Different economics — an access price is a one-time signal and a one-time transfer; a usage price is a metered stream, and it is the one that makes an artefact's cost proportional to the value it actually delivers over time. **They should be separate fields**, and this is why MYC-5's schedule is revenue-*typed* | IV | ⏸️ ⬜ **not started** — a real, concrete schema gap, not just "pricing pending" |

| ARE-11 | **★ Germination is ⊕, and that is the whole safety story.** A purchased spore is not *installed*, it is **composed**: `germinate(g, Σ_p) = Σ_p ⊕ Σ_g`, where `Σ_g = instantiate(Genome(g))` — subject to three conditions, **none of which is new machinery**: (1) **viability composes** — `V_g` holds within `V_p` (the roll-up condition, `compute_rollup`); (2) **membership stays acyclic** (Mycelium §VII; shipped limitation: `link_child` refuses a second parent, so composition is a **tree** and a germinated spore has **at most one container**); (3) **the parent's gate admits it** — the same `admit()` as every other write, `admissible = ⋀_{H∈path} admit_H`. **"There is no special install path, and there must not be one"** — complete mediation (Saltzer & Schroeder) at the market boundary. **`package_status` already encodes exactly the right lifecycle**: **SANDBOXED** is Operative §X's fork-and-replay *under the same gate*, and the transition to **LIVE** is **where the approval token belongs** (= N1/OPV-30). The three classes germinate differently, and the difference is the security model: **spore** → a new Σ by ⊕; **Enzyme** → a new element of **T**, arriving with **its own guard, written by its author** — the counterfeit surface, and §X's subject; **Symbiont** → a new element of Ω which by Operative §I Props 1–2 **cannot enlarge `R(s)` at all, only re-rank it**. The article's load-bearing sentence: ***"A Symbiont from an untrusted author is bounded by your T; an Enzyme from an untrusted author is an addition to it."*** This is why §V.4's *"the market cannot recall what it sold"* is survivable rather than alarming — **the buyer was never protected by the catalogue's judgement; they were protected by the gate on their own boundary, which the market has no path through** | IX.4 | ⏸️ 🔨 **the ⊕ half is ✅ and structural in Rust** (admit-along-the-path; a bypass is not expressible) — and the Note confirms `instantiate` / `link_child` / `provision_declared_children` / `compute_rollup` all exist, so *"§IX.4's ⊕ is real on the Sustain side."* **The lifecycle ⬜:** per the Note's item 12, `package_status` is set to `PLACED` and **never advances** — `INSTALLING`/`SANDBOXED`/`LIVE` are unreachable and there is **no install or germinate endpoint**, so *"'no special install path' is currently true in the strongest and least useful sense: there is no install path at all."* **A correctness property, not merely economic** (ties CAP-7, OPV-17) |
| ARE-12 | **Market security exposure (thin)** — the *mechanisms* belong to **M-IMM**; only the **market's exposure** is here. **(1) Counterfeit & name confusion** — content-addressing defeats *substitution* (verify the bytes) but **not** *confusion* (the wrong name, verified perfectly); typosquatting and author impersonation dominate in practice (Ohm et al. 2020). Mitigations: **namespace ownership, signature, human-readable provenance at the decision point**. **(2) Sybil authorship** (Douceur 2002) — manufacture authors, manufacture raters, manufacture adoptions; **bounded only by making identity costly** (Mycelium §VII owns this as a network invariant), and **it fails silently if identity is free**. **(3) Trust gaming** — rating rings, whitewashing, review-bombing; countered by **purchase-gated ratings, stake weighting, decay, graduated sanctions**. **(4) The malicious update** — trust earned on v1.0, payload shipped in v1.1: **the strongest argument for per-version trust and `(name, version)` write-once**. **(5) The irreducible residue** (Thompson 1984) — *a signature chains trust to an author, not to the author's toolchain*; **no provenance system closes this, it can only be moved**. **The standing structural defence:** nothing purchased can act except as an **admitted transition inside the buyer's own viable region** — the market makes good artefacts **findable**, the gate makes bad ones **inert**; merging the two deletes the property the rest depends on | X | ⏸️ ⬜ — **per-version trust** and **`(name,version)` write-once** are the two concrete unbuilt points (and `trust_score` is a dead constant today, ARE-4). The standing defence itself is **✅ structural in Rust** (complete mediation, one write path) |

##### Additions — ratified 2026-08-05 (Pawa *Additions*; Mycelium §II·§VI; Arena §IV)

*Every row here is **article-derived**, with its § cited — **no exceptions**. An earlier pass folded these from the retired `SUSTENA_WBD.md` and labelled them "not article-derived"; that was wrong, and the label is corrected. **ADD-2b was the last holdout and is no longer one:** it was recorded as "in no article body," which was itself an **under-read of Mycelium §XI** — the same error class as the Pawa miss. §X and §XI already carried the Foundation-with-zero-authority rule, `CouncilSession`/`StakeholderSession`, per-edge privileges, skins, quorum, the FLP/Paxos/BFT bounds, and proposal→vote→veto. Only the RFC-workflow / earned-weight / `sustena_core`-meta sliver was genuinely new, and it has since been written into **§XI ("RFC change control")**, so the whole row now cites canon.*

| # | Feature | § | Rust status |
|---|---|---|---|
| ADD-1 | **Juul is a KES-pegged stablecoin** — §2's gas-token posture fixes what Juul is *for*; the peg fixes its **stability**. Fiat-collateralised against the **Kenyan Shilling**, reserves in permitted instruments with a **segregated bank floor**, redeemable on M-Pesa / bank rails, so a household's costs — which are in KES — are shielded from FX and crypto volatility. Kenya now regulates the pathway (**VASP Regulations 2026**, including a stablecoin regime), and the peg is **migratable behind the settlement switch** to a CBK digital shilling or a basket at scale. *Mycelium §VI states the same requirement from the network side: Juul is a **stable** unit, not a volatile asset* | Pawa *Add.* · §2 · §6.7 · Myc §VI | ⏸️ ⬜ not started — and **§6.7's "distinct, later, human-authorized act" governs it**: this is a legal commitment as much as an engineering one |
| ADD-2 | **Governance — parameters.** Every open parameter (κ_c, κ_s, φ_p, φ_r, fee rates, the issuance schedule, the treasury budget) is a **declared, revisable Sustain**; a parameter change is an **Enzyme admitted through `admit` with its approval token**, recorded in the ledger, auditable by replay — *"the on-chain-governance analog, expressed in Sustena's own construct"* (= **PAWA-11**) | Pawa §6.5 | 🔨 **MECHANISM ✅ IN RUST 2026-08-18** (`governance.rs`) — a parameter change IS an ordinary Enzyme: same registry, same approval token, **bounds as ordinary invariants**, recorded as `event.governance.parameter_changed` (carrying **from** and **to**), and **replayable through `replay_under`**. **No governance engine was written.** ★★ Coverage is **four parameters** — `κ_c`/`κ_s`, governed end to end after `pawa::compute_pawa` and both constants were **deleted** so nothing prices from a const; and (2026-08-18) **`issuance_rate`**, PAWA-8’s, which is this mechanism’s **first outside customer** and cost one const, one accessor and one line — no new Enzyme, no new gate. ★ The **issuance schedule TYPE** stays declared-at-spec rather than governed, for the same scalar-fit reason: a decaying schedule needs a **clock** this core does not have, and a target-rate one would make the governed value a *target* with the rate derived from ledger state. ★★ And (2026-08-18) **`κ_att`**, OPV-7's attention coefficient — `attention::KAPPA` **demoted** and `Aperture::cost()` **deleted**, the third constant this mechanism has removed. Its **allocation** stays host-supplied per operative, deliberately: one global scalar would be the wrong shape. The **royalty schedule** is a named follow-on: governing it needs a **sum-to-100** bound `ParameterSpec` (one scalar) cannot express. |
| ADD-2b | **Governance — RFC change control.** *"Changing the network's own rules is itself a Transition, so it is admitted like any other."* A change to `sustena_core` — a shared parameter, an Enzyme, a Sustain definition — is raised as an **RFC**, discussed, put to a **`CouncilSession`** (plus a **`StakeholderSession`** wherever interests genuinely conflict), and **on quorum finalized and anchored**: the proposal is an Enzyme, `admit` **with its approval token** is the gate, the ledger records it, auditable by replay. **Weight is earned, not bought** — voting weight rises with **merged contributions and Arena reputation** (the same earned trust the royalty economy already meters) and may carry a **bounded, declared** pawa/Juul stake, **capped so that no holding buys control**: reputation primary, stake a minor revisable axis, **the person-first default holds**. **Graduated rights — proposer / reviewer / maintainer — ARE skins**: named bundles of **edge** privileges, least-privilege by default (so per **CAP-17** the weight attaches to the **membership edge**, not the member). And **the convening network-scale Symbiont has, per §I, the authority of any Symbiont: none — it runs the process, it does not decide it** | Myc §XI · §X · Pawa §6.5 | ⏸️ ⬜ not started — needs `StakeholderSession` (⬜) on top of the existing `CouncilSession`, plus the approval token (**N1/OPV-30**) it shares a gate with |
| ADD-3 | **Discovery — a hybrid behind ONE resolution interface**, and the article is sharper than "registry then DHT": the correct design is *"**not** registry-or-DHT but a hybrid behind one resolution interface"* — `resolve(name) → holder`, so the backend changes **without touching a single caller**. A registry/index serves the fast common case (**10⁰–10³**: O(1) lookup, O(n) state at the registry); a **Kademlia DHT plus gossip** carries scale and resilience as n crosses ~10³ (**10³–10⁶**: O(log n) hops, O(log n) state — Kademlia's XOR metric makes the routing table self-maintaining from ordinary traffic); **Babychain anchoring (§VI) sits above both**. Build the interface and the registry now with the overlay **mocked behind it**, so the DHT is a drop-in. **The threshold is a design trigger, not a guess:** *a registry stops being correct the moment its single operator's availability becomes a network-wide invariant — when the registry's failure would violate `V_net`* | Myc §II | ⏸️ ⬜ not started (this **supersedes MYC-1**'s generic "addressing and discovery") |
| ADD-4 | **Settlement rides the layered stack, behind a swappable adapter** — Sustena executes locally; **Babychains** are the L2 (aggregation, rollup, zk-proofs); **Ethereum is the finalizer**, receiving only state-root hashes. *The ⊕ hierarchy **is** the rollup batching structure* — a homestead folds its habitats, a Babychain folds its Sustains, and only key transactions and periodic roots reach L1 (§V's value/information law read at the settlement layer). Two disciplines make it shippable: **(i)** the whole L1/L2 stack sits behind a settlement interface with a **mock** — pawa/Juul run as live utility tokens today behind an adapter to Ethereum standards (ERC-20 / rollup / finalizer), the chain switched on later **with no rewrite**, the same swap-behind-a-stable-interface pattern as ADD-3; **(ii)** **finality is layered, so latency is too** — local transactions final in milliseconds, global finality at the anchoring cadence, and most transaction classes never need L1 at all | Pawa *Add.* · §6 · Myc §VI | ⏸️ ⬜ not started (= the build strategy for **CAP-16**) |
| ADD-5 | **Correction to the Sustena Note** — *"none of pawa/Juul exists yet"* **predates Slice 15**: the **pawa meter is built and wired into `execute_operator`**. The rest of the additive plan stands | Pawa *Add.* | ✅ app-layer (= **PAWA-1**; status already correct) |

**Summary:** the economy layer is **⏸️ deferred wholesale**, and the article draws the deferral line itself (PAWA-13): the meter, ledger, treasury accounting and gating are buildable now; **issuing a real transferable token is a separate, later, human-authorized act**. What already exists is the **meter** (Slice 15, wired into `execute_operator`, coefficients honestly uncalibrated) and a real append-only `PawaLedger` — whose `charge()` runs the **superseded four-way split** and **has zero callers**. **A main-body sweep on 2026-08-12** — a section-by-section cross-check of all 18 articles against this WBD — found **17 modules faithful and two genuine gaps, both in the Arena**: `ARE-11` (§IX.4, germination is ⊕) and `ARE-12` (§X, market security exposure), added here. Four items are worth carrying forward even while deferred, because they are **correctness rather than economics**: **ARE-2** (publish is a second write path), **ARE-11** (germination has no special install path — and the approval token belongs on the SANDBOXED→LIVE transition), **MYC-6** (a grant that mints rather than transfers makes Sybil registration profitable, and must be *declared*), and **MYC-5**'s conservation property (integer-floor remainder, assigned to the validator share, is what makes the split exactly conserving). **As of 2026-08-12 that property is finally checkable**: `CON-7`'s conservation law is the mechanism a royalty split's exact-conservation would be declared against. **ADD-1–5** carry the 2026-08-05 Additions — the KES peg, parameter governance, **RFC change control**, the discovery hybrid and the settlement adapter — **all article-derived with their § cited, none excepted**. Two prior mislabels are corrected here: the peg/schedule/settlement stack (folded from a retired tracker, actually Pawa's own *Additions*) and **ADD-2b**, recorded as "in no article body" when it was an under-read of **Mycelium §X·§XI** — now cited, and completed in canon by §XI's *"RFC change control"*.

### Capstone / cross-cutting

#### M-CAP · The whole machine  ✅ read
*Article: `3B1B — Capstone - Formal Execution.md` (GAIA). The assembly — one object, seventeen times. Not a module to build: the argument that the others compose, plus the seams where leverage concentrates.*

**Status: this section is the assembly view. Its build content is the SEAMS — and the gate seam is where Rust has already banked the largest structural wins.**

| # | Feature | § | Rust status |
|---|---|---|---|
| CAP-1 | **The primitive restated; the module map — one object, seventeen times** — every article is `Σ = ⟨B,S,V,T,⊕⟩` at a different scale | I·II | ✅ the premise the Rust core is built on |
| CAP-2 | **The recursion theorem + the four lifts** — the construction, the theorem, and the fixpoint "stated without mysticism", with **two honest caveats** on it | III | 🔨 ⊕/ρ ✅ in Rust; the theorem is the design's justification, not code |
| CAP-3 | **The six-layer frame** — Substrate (Events&Time) → Definition (Sustain, Enzyme, Constraint, Embroidery) → Boundary (Ingest) → Cognition (Monitor, Tenet, Symbiont, Multiparty) → Surface (Curated UI, Editing) → Governance (Controller) | IV | ✅ **this WBD's own layering** |
| CAP-4 | **The dependency order** `Events&Time → Embroidery → Sustain → Enzyme → Constraint → Ingest → Monitor → (Curated UI ∥ Tenet → Symbiont → Multiparty)`; **Editing after the Enzyme path**; **the Controller is NOT a late layer — it threads through from the first gate check onward** | IV | ✅ **the Rust build order followed it**: fold → state → rules → operators+gate → council |
| CAP-5 | **Mycelium, Arena and Immune are not a seventh layer** — Mycelium and Arena are Σ at larger scales; **Immune is a property of the Boundary and Definition layers**, not a member of any | IV | ✅ (recorded — this is why M-IMM's items land inside the gate) |
| CAP-6 | **The trace — the composition proof** — one M-Pesa message end to end, **every engine appearing exactly once**, using only machinery the specs already contain. *"The trace is the argument: if any step required machinery no spec provides, the series would have a hole"* | V | ✅ the trace runs today in Python (capture → τ → dedupe → gate → fold → compose) |
| CAP-7 | **★★ SEAM 1 — the gate has SIX duties** — (1) admit operators to live state, (2) enforce invariants and post-conditions, (3) **check the approval token**, (4) **replay sandboxed simulations under the same rules**, (5) **gate edit authority**, (6) **mediate the boundary firewall F**. *"The single highest-leverage object in the design. Build it once, and six obligations across five sections are discharged"* | VI.1 | 🔨 **Five of the six §VI.1 duties are banked in Rust** — 1 ✅ admit, 2 ✅ invariants + post-conditions **and now `D(s,s')`** (`transition.rs`), 3 ✅ the bound single-use approval token (`approval.rs`), 4 ✅ sandbox-under-the-same-gate (structural: one execution path), 5 ✅ edit authority (`editing.rs`). **Only 6, the boundary firewall `F`, remains** — blocked on `μ`, the membership predicate (= CON-1's third family, SUS-*). The two later-found duties (CAP-8) are separate |
| CAP-8 | **Two further gate duties the later articles found** (flagged as assembly, not §9.3 canon) — (7) the **LLM task-class budget** is an ordinary constraint, one more predicate on the same seam; (8) **`publish` is a route, not an Enzyme**, so the seam has a **market-side duty** too | VI.1 | ⬜ both not started (7 ⏸️ economy-deferred; 8 = ARE-2) |
| CAP-9 | **★ SEAM 2 — fork+sim is used three ways** — Tenet's scenario replay, the Symbiont sandbox, and Editing's simulate-before-commit. **One mechanism, three consumers, and the correctness condition is that all three run under the same gate** — which is exactly where the Python code diverges | VI.2 | ✅ **structural in Rust** — there is one execution path, so all three consumers must ride it (this is the concrete form of backlog #12) |
| CAP-10 | **SEAM 3 — the log sits under three things at once** — state (as a fold), definitions (as versioned history), audit (what happened, when, from whom); and per TOLERANCE it is **immune memory**: the substrate on which *this has happened before* and *this has never happened before* both become answerable | VI.3 | 🔨 state-as-fold ✅; **definitions-as-versioned-history ⬜ (= EDIT-7)**; audit/immune-memory reads ⬜ |
| CAP-11 | **SEAM 4 — Embroidery is the single declaration source** — S dimensions, V invariants, guards and effects, utilities and weights, widget schemas and bindings, connectors, domains. *"One place to say what is true — which is the only way the type-checker can be one type-checker"* | VI.4 | 🔨 Rust has **one** predicate parser/evaluator and a typed schema (R2 #14) — the property this seam is about; the **authoring language ⬜** (= M-DSL) |
| CAP-12 | **SEAM 5 — provenance threads the whole length** — device tokens → event principals → approval decisions. *"Break the thread anywhere and the audit becomes a list of assertions"* | VI.5 | 🔨 event `provenance` ✅ (R2 #17 legacy marker) + principals ✅ (R2 #10); **device tokens → approval decisions ⬜** |
| CAP-13 | **Behaviour varies by domain — the domain × engine matrix** (Observe/Model/Learn/Respond × clear/complicated/complex/chaotic); **domain detection is a Monitor function** (a shift is a regime change — what CUSUM exists to catch); **disorder is a first-class reading**, surfaced as *confidence over the domain estimate* | VII | ✅ **DONE** — `core/domain_engine.rs`, 11 tests, over SUS-16's `domain_map` and `cynefin.rs`. ★★★ **All sixteen cells are declared, and the two that say DO NOT are why it is a matrix and not a sentence**: `Model` and `Learn` **refuse** in a chaotic regime. A model of chaos is a confident wrong answer and a person acts on it; learning there fits noise that then travels into every later decision. **Refusing is not degrading** — a "reduced-confidence model" is the same wrong number in a smaller font — so `Behaviour::Refuse` has no value in it to read. ★★★ **Disorder arrives exactly as the row asks — as confidence over the estimate**: `Estimate` has no constructor that omits confidence, and a `Known(Complicated)` at 51% is **disorder however definite its label looks**, because the label is not evidence of itself. Below the floor the matrix cannot be indexed and the answer is `Surface`, never a default row — picking one anyway is how an unrecognised situation gets handled as a familiar one. ★★ `Model × Complex` is `Bounded`, carrying OPV-13's `H*` as its limit, because "it works, with a limit" and "it works" are different promises. ★★ `Observe` runs in all four — it assumes nothing about the regime — which is why it is what is left when the rest stops. ⬜ The *classifier* half (a Monitor that produces the estimate from CUSUM regime-change detection) stays open; this row builds what consumes it, and `cynefin.rs`'s "supplied, never computed here" discipline is unchanged. |
| CAP-14 | **★ The honest tension at assembly scale** — *"Most of Sustena is complicated-domain machinery… a household and a business are complex systems."* The complex row is deliberately **not** the complicated row with more compute; Ashby: variety by **nesting** grows combinatorially where variety by **enumeration** runs out — *"the load-bearing reason the primitive is recursive"* | VII·IX.3 | ✅ recorded as the design's own stated limit |
| CAP-15 | **Two surfaces, one truth** — Mycelium and Orchie are **projections of the same object differing only in budget** | VIII.1 | ⬜ Rust (✅ app-layer) |
| CAP-16 | **Settlement: Local → Babychain → finalizer** — each Sustain executes locally and almost all activity stays there; a **Babychain** aggregates and **anchors periodically**, committing the **state-root hash** (optionally with a validity proof) rather than the transactions; *"Ethereum as finalizer does not run the system; it finalises its truth"* | VIII.2 | ⏸️ economy-deferred |
| CAP-17 | **Governance — local defines, global enforces** — a DAO is a Sustain with embedded consensus; **CouncilSession** for a single-human Sustain, **StakeholderSession** added when interests are genuinely in tension; **authority attaches to the membership EDGE, not the member**; a **skin** is a named bundle of edge privileges (RBAC); bounds: FLP, and `k ≥ 3f+1` | VIII.3 | ✅ **edge-authority + skins built in Rust (R2 #10)**; **consensus ✅ built 2026-08-16** (= MUL-7/8/9 — quorum/Paxos with `2q > k` structural, FLP and `k ≥ 3f+1` recorded as the bounds they are), and EDIT-8's `CouncilMint` now derives a shared-definition quorum from a real `Decision` rather than asserting one; StakeholderSession ⬜ |
| CAP-18 | **Foundation and Institute — the no-special-power rule is structural** — complete mediation, no bypass. *"A privileged-Symbiont exception is not a feature with a risk; it is the deletion of the property that makes the rest of the argument true"* | VIII.4 | ✅ **structural in Rust** (one path, no privileged bypass) |
| CAP-19 | **The thesis, made precise** — *"Any system that can be described can be modeled, simulated, and optimized before execution"*; the loop **DESCRIBE → MODEL → SIMULATE → OPTIMISE → EXECUTE → OBSERVE**, closed, forever | IX.1 | 🔨 DESCRIBE/EXECUTE ✅; **SIMULATE partial, OPTIMISE ⬜, OBSERVE ⬜** (= M-TEN, M-MON, M-CTL) |
| CAP-20 | **Why one recursive primitive is the economical route** — the argument is about **interfaces, and it is countable** | IX.3 | ✅ the justification for the whole build |

**Summary:** the Capstone is not a module to build but the **map of where leverage concentrates**, and it names the two seams Rust has already banked: **the gate** (four of six duties discharged, structurally) and **fork+sim under one gate** (a bypass is not expressible). The remaining gate duties are the **bound approval token** (OPV-30), **edit authority** (EDIT-8) and the **firewall F** (CON-1) — and the article adds two more found later: the **LLM policy predicate** and **publish-as-an-Enzyme** (ARE-2). The build order this WBD follows is the article's own (CAP-4), including its two insistences: **Editing comes after the Enzyme path**, and **the Controller is not a late layer**. CAP-14 is the honest frame for everything: most of the machinery is complicated-domain, most households are complex, and the matrix (CAP-13) is how the design faces that rather than papering over it.

## New gaps found by the final eight reads

*Not on `R2_BACKLOG.md`. Recorded here so the backlog can absorb them on its next pass; each is marked ★ or ⚠ in its own module above.*

| # | Gap | Module | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | **The approval token — ✅ SHIPPED IN RUST 2026-08-12** (`sustena-core/src/approval.rs`). Was grep-0 everywhere. Built bound to `(o,θ)`, single-use and expiring, with the trace invariant `sim ≺ vote ≺ approve` enforced by **move semantics** rather than a runtime scan — prefix-closed, hence a safety property, hence structural. **Still open in Python**, recorded as a documented rust-ahead divergence | OPV-30/31, EDIT-8, CAP-7 | The gate's third duty. One object discharges obligations in three articles |
| N2 | **`publish` is a route, not an Enzyme** — a **second write path into the Arena**, the exact failure complete mediation forbids | ARE-2, CAP-8 | A seventh gate duty the Capstone found after §9.3 |
| N3 | **A definition edit destroys its predecessor — ✅ RESOLVED IN RUST 2026-08-12** (`sustena-core/src/version.rs`). Was an in-place `UPDATE … SET spec_json` with a bare counter, so rollback had nothing to roll back *to*. Now an append-only DAG with a head pointer, declared inverses over the typed taxonomy, and three-valued rollback whose `Total` cannot carry a Δ. **Still open in Python**, recorded as a documented rust-ahead divergence | EDIT-7 | Blocks the whole rollback algebra |
| N4 | **`edit.operator_spec` lets `pawa_cost` and `license_tier` be changed at runtime, free, with no authority check** — and mutates the in-memory registry, so the change is process-local and lost on restart | EDIT-14 | *Changing an Enzyme's price costs less than using it* |
| N5 | **`edit.state_patch`'s `remove` writes `None` instead of deleting** — under a typed schema that deposits a type violation into state | EDIT-15 | Rust's organisational closure would refuse it |
| N6 | **Live bug — `NetworkZone.jsx:47`** — `owner_ids?.[0]` on the unsubstituted token `"{{owner_ids}}"` indexes the *string* and yields `"{"`, which is truthy, so the fallback never fires and `"{"` is posted as the `user_id` provisioning child sustains | EDIT-16 | Wrong ownership on real children today; nothing refused, nothing logged |
| N7 | **The `domain` key collision** — `operatives.<n>.domain` is a subject-matter tag list; the article's `dom` is the **Cynefin axis**. Reusing the key makes one of them inexpressible | OPV-16, CAP-13 | Cheap to avoid now, expensive after specs ship |
| N8 | **A grant that mints is not a grant that transfers** — a transfer conserves and is treasury-bounded; a mint is unbounded and **makes Sybil registration directly profitable**. Both are legitimate; *only one is legitimate undeclared* | MYC-6 | ⏸️ economy, but it is a correctness property, not a pricing choice |
| N9 | **`compose(r)`'s recency window is a hard-coded `limit=25`** — a widget whose triggering event has scrolled out cannot be summoned at all | UI-4 | A policy living in a call argument |
| N10 | **Urgency returns 0.0 when `allocated ≤ 0`** — an **unfunded pocket can never be urgent**, which is backwards | UI-7 | ✅ **RESOLVED IN RUST 2026-08-17** — and by removal rather than repair: UI-1 replaced the proxy with `d(s,V)`, so **there is no denominator left to be zero**. ★ A conformance case runs this exact scenario (an unfunded pocket spent from, driving state through a wall `V` declares) and it reads a **non-zero** urgency. ★★ Still open **on the Python side**, where the proxy is what ships — and worth noting the honest limit either way: if `V` declares no relevant wall, `d(s,V)` is legitimately 0 there, which `UrgencyBasis::Undeclared` now reports as *silence, not safety* rather than papering over. |

**Two cheap, high-value items** surfaced that are *not* new gaps but are newly costed: **runway** (`R(s)=min{k: s_k∉V}` — one policy, one forward scan, OPV-19) and the **branching-ratio criticality read** (σ̂ is a read over the log's `causes`, OPV-14). Both become available the moment V is a region and `causes` is on the event record.
